PREFACE 

Six years having passed since the publication of the 

Eighth Edition of Honeyman's "NEW JERSEY JUSTICE," 

it is deemed wise at this time to publish a Supplement to the work. Since Mr. Honeyman has passed to his reward, the work has been undertaken by the writer. 

This Supplement contains the amendments and supplements to the matter contained in the 1933 work. Much new material has been added. 

The writer has tried to follow the alphabetical outline formulated by Mr. Honeyman. Hence, material of the same nature is likely to be found under the same general topic as the topic under which it is to be found in the old book; at any rate, a large index has been placed in the Supplement, so that the reader should be able to find his material without difficulty. It will be noted that, in this index, there are many references to the index of the original volume as well as to the text thereof. 

The reader is cautioned that, where a contradiction exists between the 1933 work and the Supplement, the principle set forth in the Supplement is to be followed: a new law repeals the old law on the subject. 

The Small Cause Court Act has been placed in the front of the book: the writer considers this repetition necessary as a result of the revising of the statutes in 

1937. Justices of the peace, recorders, magistrates, etc., should examine this Act to discover any changes which have been made in the principal statute governing their courts; for example, actions in the Small Cause Court today are styled "actions at law," whereas, prior to the 1937 Revision, actions were styled either "actions on

vi 

PREFACE 

contract" or "actions in tort," as the case might have been.

Forms will be found throughout the books. Names, addresses, dates, and so forth have been used in these forms, all of which names and so forth are fictitious. 

The reader will find that many of the forms in the original text can be used in the new statutory actions. 

The writer has made numerous references to the original text. Such original text is the eighth or 1933 edition and no other. This book is not a supplement to the older editions.

Though many matters have been explained in detail in this book, the reader will find that, in many instances, he has been given only a reference to the statutes. It is his duty to consult the statutes in these cases. An examination of the statutes and cases is always in order and should be made whenever possible.

For the convenience of those readers who are not acquainted with law books and their terminology, the writer sets forth the following table to explain the meaning of the abbreviations and letters which the reader will find in the citations: 

Rev. 1937-Revised Statutes of 1937

Stat. Serv.-Statutory Service of the year desig nated

P. L.-Pamphlet Laws of the year designated 

N. J. L.-New Jersey Law Reports N. J. E.-New Jersey Equity Reports N. J. Misc.-New Jersey Miscellaneous Reports N. J. L. J.-New Jersey Law Journal Atl. Rep.-Atlantic Reporter 

Note: The number preceding the abbreviation of the reports is the volume number, while the number following the abbreviation is the page number; thus, 105 N. J. 

L. 33 means Volume 105 of the New Jersey Law Reports at page 33.

PREFACE 

vii The writer hopes that this book will be used, particularly by the members of the minor judiciary, and that the book will prove to be an assistance in the administration of justice in this State. 

Audubon, N. J.,

January, 1940. 

JOSEPH PIERCE LODGE.

SUPPLEMENT 

TO 

HONEYMAN'S "NEW JERSEY JUSTICE" I.  Revision of 1937 with Supplements 1938 and 1939 

R.S. 2:9-1. For the purpose of determining the number of justices of the peace which each township or ward may elect at each annual election, the abstract of the last previous federal census shall be conclusive evidence of the number of inhabitants in each township or ward. 

R.S. 2:9-1a. (1938 Stat. Serv.). Whenever, in any ward in any municipality of this State, an election has heretofore been held to fill a vacancy in the office of justice of the peace and at such election a majority of the votes cast were in favor of electing a justice of the peace, then the provisions of such election shall be deemed to apply to and be in operation and force in any such municipality.

R.S. 2:9-1b. (1938 Stat. Serv.). All elections held in any ward in any municipality as aforesaid are hereby validated and confirmed, notwithstanding any defect in the notice of such election, or in the calling of the same, or in the method or manner of submission or certification thereof.

R.S. 2:9-2. The secretary of state shall, from the statement of the number of votes cast for each candidate for the office of justice of the peace filed with him by 

[Honeyman's Justice Supp.]—1

the election board as provided by sections 19:17-3 and 

19:17-4 of the title Elections, determine the results of the elections and notify the clerks of the respective counties thereof, on or before March first next following the election; and the respective clerks shall, on or before 

April first next ensuing, notify by mail or otherwise each person so determined to have been elected a justice of the peace in his county.

R.S. 2:9-3. No person who has been convicted of a misdemeanor or higher crime shall qualify as a justice of the peace.

R.S. 2:9-4. Except as provided by section 2:9-3 of this title, each person notified of his election as provided by section 2:9-2 of this title shall, if he desires to do so, qualify on or before May first following his notification, by making an affidavit that he has not been convicted of a misdemeanor or higher crime, by giving the bond required by section 2:9-11 of this title and by subscribing to an oath of office, on blank forms to be furnished for that purpose by the secretary of state, including, in addition to the statutory oath of office, both the federal and state oath of allegiance. The person so qualifying shall also pay, for the use of the state and the county, the fees provided by section 22:2-19 of the title Fees and Costs. 

On or before May first of each year the respective county clerks shall file with the secretary of state the affidavits and oaths of all justices of the peace who shall have qualified as herein provided, and shall transmit therewith the fees provided for the state. 

R.S. 2:9-5. The secretary of state shall, immediately after the affidavits and oaths and the state fees provided by section 22:2-19 of the title Fees and Costs have been transmitted to him, present to the governor the record of the persons qualifying as herein provided, and the 

[Honeyman's Justice Supp.]

governor shall, within twenty days thereafter, issue commissions to the persons so qualifying, which shall be dated as of May first as provided by the constitution. 

R.S. 2:9-6. Each justice of the peace, to whom a commission has issued as provided by section 2:9-5 of this title, may perform the duties of his office from and after May first of the year in which the commission is issued. 

R.S. 2:9-7. If an elected justice of the peace neglects or refuses to qualify as provided by this chapter for two months after the date of his commission, his election shall be void. 

R.S. 2:9-8. A justice of the peace entering upon the exercise of his office before taking the oaths and filing the bond required shall forfeit to the state for each offense the sum of one hundred dollars. 

R.S. 2:9-9. A justice of the peace may resign his office by transmitting to the governor or person administering the government his resignation in writing and a copy thereof to the clerk of the municipality for which the justice was elected, which copy the clerk shall file in his office. Thereupon the office shall become vacant. 

R.S. 2:9-10. Neither any justice of the peace nor any person who, by virtue of his office, exercises the powers of a justice of the peace shall be granted a license to keep an inn or tavern or to sell spirituous, vinous, malt or brewed liquors.

If any person holding a license to keep an inn or tavern or to sell spirituous, vinous, malt or brewed liquors shall be elected or appointed a justice of the peace or an officer with the powers of a justice of the peace, and shall qualify as such, his license shall become and be absolutely void. 

R.S. 2:9-11. Every person elected to the office of justice of the peace in any of the counties of this state, and

eligible to qualify therefor, shall give bond to the state of New Jersey, in such sum as a judge of the court of common pleas of the county for which he is elected shall deem sufficient, not less than five hundred nor more than three thousand dollars, with sufficient surety approved by the judge, and conditioned for the payment on demand to the person entitled or authorized to receive the same of all moneys coming into his hands as justice of the peace during his continuance in office. If individual and not corporate surety is given, the sureties shall be freeholders. The bond shall be for the benefit of any person concerned or aggrieved and shall be filed in the office of the county clerk of the proper county.

The court of common pleas of the proper county may, from time to time, upon application in writing by a person concerned or aggrieved, order a prosecution of the bond at the expense of the applicant. 

R.S. 2:9-12. Every justice of the peace shall report monthly, during his term of office, on the first day of the month, to the treasurer of his county, the amount of fines imposed and received by him during the preceding month, and shall, at the same time, pay over all fines so by him imposed and received, and such fines so paid over as are declared by law to be for the use of the county shall be held by the county treasurer for the use of the county.

If any justice of the peace shall fail to report and make payment as directed by this section for thirty days after notice from the treasurer so to do, such justice shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor. 

R.S. 2:9–13. All fines which may be authorized under any proceeding before any justice of the peace, and which are not appropriated by law to any distinct use, shall be and are appropriated and made payable to the

county treasurer of the county in which the justice of the peace may reside, for the use of the county. 

R.S. 2:9-14. Each justice of the peace shall use a seal which shall bear in circular form the inscription "the small cause court of the county of New

Jersey," together with his name and the state, ward or township for which he is elected, and he shall, before entering upon the duties of his office, file with the county clerk of his county a certificate with the impression of his seal thereon. 

R.S. 2:9-15. Each justice of the peace shall, except as provided by section 2:8-46 of this title, have authority to hold a court within the county for which he is elected, which court shall be known as "the small cause court," shall be a court of record, shall have jurisdiction over actions or proceedings of a civil nature at law as hereinafter provided, and, for the purpose of hearing, trying and determining such actions or proceedings, shall have such authority and power as is usual in courts of record of this state, except the power to punish for contempt.

R.S. 2:9-16. Subject to the limitations stated in section 2:8-46 of this title, the jurisdiction of a justice of the peace holding a small cause court shall extend to: a. All actions or proceedings of a civil nature at law, where the matter in dispute does not, exclusive of costs, exceed the sum or value of two hundred dollars, although the cause of action did not arise in the county in which the court is held; b. Proceedings between landlords and tenants and actions of forcible entry and detainer; c. Actions or proceedings to recover every sum of money or penalty, not exceeding two hundred dollars, recoverable by virtue of any law of this state or ordinance of any municipality.

A justice of the peace holding a small cause court shall not have any jurisdiction over actions of replevin, slander, trespass for assault, battery or imprisonment, or any action wherein the title to real estate shall come in question.

R.S. 2:9-17. The territorial jurisdiction of each jus tice of the peace shall, except as otherwise provided by section 2:8-46 of this title, be coextensive with the boundaries of the county for which he is elected and commissioned. 

R.S. 2:9-18. Where the amount really due or claimed exceeds, exclusive of costs, the sum or value of two hundred dollars, the plaintiff may elect to bring his action in a small cause court for a sum not exceeding two hundred dollars and costs; and judgment on the merits in an action so brought shall bar any other action on the same claim in any other court. 

R.S. 2:9-19. When the amount really due or recoverable upon any bond, bill, note or other contract in writing does not exceed, exclusive of costs, the sum or value of two hundred dollars, such amount may be recovered in a small cause court and judgment shall be rendered therefor without regard to any kind of penalty expressed therein.

R.S. 2:9-20. If the defendant files as a set-off or recoupment a demand against the plaintiff for more than two hundred dollars, and, at the trial, it shall be proved that a balance exceeding two hundred dollars, is due to the defendant, the action shall be dismissed, unless the defendant consents to take a judgment for two hundred dollars and costs in full settlement of his claim. 

R.S. 2:9-21. No justice of the peace, unless duly licensed as an attorney at law of this state, or constable, shall appear and prosecute or defend any action brought

in any small cause court, unless such justice or constable shall be one of the parties on record in the cause. 

Any justice or constable violating this section shall forfeit fifty dollars, recoverable in an action at law with costs, in any court of competent jurisdiction, in the name and for the use of any person prosecuting therefor. 

R.S. 2:9-22. The constables of the several wards and townships in any county shall be the ministerial officers of the small cause courts. 

R.S. 2:9-23. No justice of the peace shall issue any summons, writ of attachment or other process out of the small cause court over which he presides for or on behalf of any person for whom he is agent or attorney in fact, or take any fee or reward for any such service. 

R.S. 2:9-24. No justice of the peace shall take any fee or reward for receiving or paying over to the person entitled thereto money received by him from a party in an action or proceeding in the small cause court over which he presides. 

R.S. 2:9-25. No justice of the peace shall charge, take or receive fees in excess of the amounts allowed by section 22:2-51 of the title Fees and Costs, or otherwise allowed by law. 

R.S. 2:9–26. A justice of the peace violating any of the provisions of sections 2:9-23 to 2:9-25 of this title, shall, for each offense, be liable to a penalty of fifty dollars, recoverable, with costs, by any person aggrieved who may sue for the same. 

GENERAL PROVISIONS 

R.S. 2:33-1. As used in this chapter, unless the context clearly indicates otherwise, the word "justice" means a justice of the peace holding a small cause court.

R.S. 2:33-2. A body politic or corporate of this or any other state may sue and be sued in a small cause court in the same manner as a natural person. 

R.S. 2:33-3. Parties to an action or proceeding in a small cause court may agree to enter the same therein without process, whereupon the court shall proceed to final judgment and execution as though process had issued and been duly served. 

R.S. 2:33-4. A justice whose term of office has expired or who has resigned his office, may proceed to the final determination of any cause pending in his small cause court and undetermined at the time he ceases to hold office, and make return to all writs, orders or rules directed to him and issuing out of any court of this state, as though his commission had not expired or he had not resigned.

PROCESS 

R.S. 2:33-5. The writs, precepts and process issued out of any small cause court shall run in and through the county for which the justice is elected and commissioned, and he may, in causes pending before him in such court, award writs of subpoenas for witnesses into other counties of this state. 

R.S. 2:33-6. Every summons, subpoena, commitment, warrant, writ or process issuing out of a small cause court shall be tested the day of issue, and shall be signed and sealed by the justice issuing the same, with the seal of his court. No process issued out of a small cause court shall be valid without the impression of his seal. 

R.S. 2:33-7. The constables of the several wards and townships in any county shall execute within the county all precepts, summons, warrants, writs and other process issuing out of a small cause court, and to them or any of

them directed and delivered and make return thereof, and shall perform all matters, acts and things appertaining to their duties as officers of the small cause court. 

R.S. 2:33-8. No constable or other officer, authorized to serve a subpoena, summons or other mesne process issued out of a small cause court, shall be required to serve such process until his legal fees and mileage for such service are paid to him.

R.S. 2:33-9. The first process to compel appearance shall be a summons or warrant in the nature of a capias ad respondendum. 

R.S. 2:33-10. No warrant shall issue out of a small cause court against the body of a female. 

R.S. 2:33-11. No justice shall sign his name to a blank summons or warrant, or allow a constable or other person to fill in blanks therein, unless the same be done by special direction of the justice and in his presence, and every such process so signed shall be void. 

R.S. 2:33-12. The justice shall enter in the body of each summons or warrant, issued by virtue of this chapter, the amount demanded, and indorse the same, with costs, on the summons or warrant. 

R.S. 2:33-13. If a defendant in an action or proceeding in a small cause court pays, without further proceedings in the cause, the amount demanded and stated in a summons or warrant, with costs, the constable shall receive the same; and his receipt given therefor shall be a full discharge from the demand and costs to the defendant making payment. 

R.S. 2:33-14. A constable, receiving money as provided in section 2:33-13 of this title and not paying the same over to the justice issuing the process, or to the plaintiff or his legal representative, within fifteen days after his

receipt thereof, shall be liable therefor to the plaintiff or his legal representative, recoverable in an action at law in a court of competent jurisdiction, with interest and double costs. 

R.S. 2:33-15. Each summon shall be made returnable between the hours of nine A. M. and three P. M., both inclusive, shall specify a time and place certain, not less than five nor more than fifteen days from the date thereof, for the appearance of the defendant, and shall be served as hereinafter provided. 

R.S. 2:33-16. The summons shall be served on the de fendant, at least five days before the time of appearance mentioned therein, by reading it and delivering a copy thereof to him, if he is found, and, if not found, by leaving a copy thereof at his usual place of abode, with some person of his family over the age of fourteen years, who shall be informed of its contents.

R.S. 2:33-17. The constable serving a summons shall indorse thereon a return of the time and manner of his execution thereof and sign his name thereto. 

R.S. 2:33-18. If a defendant in an action or proceeding in a small cause court is a corporation, the summons may be served as follows: a. If a domestic corporation, upon the president or head officer or agent in charge of its principal office in this state, either personally or by leaving a copy thereof at his usual place of abode, at least six days before the return date thereof. If the president, head officer or agent cannot be found for service and has no usual place of abode in the county, the summons may be served upon the secretary or clerk of the corporation, if there be any in the county, or, if none, then upon one of its directors in the county, or, if there be no director in the county, upon the agent in charge of any office maintained in the

county, either personally or by leaving a copy thereof at his usual place of abode in the county, at least six days before the return day thereof; b. If a foreign corporation, upon the agent in charge of its principal office in the state or upon any officer thereof, either personally or by leaving a copy thereof at his usual place of abode, or by leaving a copy thereof at the office, depot or usual place of business of the corporation within the county, with any person in charge thereof. 

R.S. 2:33-19. Plaintiff, in an action commenced by summons, may deliver to the justice his state of demand against the defendant at the time the summons issues, and furnish as many copies thereof as there are defendants to be served. The state of demand shall thereupon be attached to the summons and one of the copies shall be attached to and served with each copy of the summons, and the return of the constable upon the summons shall be proof of the service of the state of demand upon the defendant. 

R.S. 2:33-20. If a constable's return to a summons or warrant is defective, he may, if in fact he has served the same as provided by this chapter, amend his return on or before the trial of the cause, so as to make it conform to the fact, whether or not defendant appears or objects. 

R.S. 2:33-21. A warrant for arrest shall issue out of a small cause court only in actions upon contract and upon proof by affidavit or otherwise to the satisfaction of the justice, that there is a debt or demand founded upon contract, express or implied, due plaintiff from defendant, specifying the nature and particulars of the debt or demand, and establishing one or more of the following particulars: a.

Defendant fraudulently contracted the debt or in-

curred the obligation respecting which the action is brought; or b. Defendant is about to remove any of his property out of the state, with intent to defraud his creditors; or c. Defendant has assigned, removed or disposed of, or is about to assign, remove or dispose of, any of his property, with intent to defraud his creditors. 

R.S. 2:33-22. Upon proof made as provided by section 2:33-21 of this title the justice shall make and subscribe an order that a warrant issue against the defendant for such amount as the proof justifies and requires. 

Before the warrant issues the justice shall file the order and the proof.

The warrant commanding defendant's arrest shall be returnable forthwith on the day of the service thereof, and the constable serving the warrant shall indorse thereon the execution thereof and sign his name thereto. 

R.S. 2:33-23. The justice making an order pursuant to section 2:33-22 of this title shall furnish to defendant or his agent, upon request therefor, a certified copy of the order or affidavit upon which the warrant for arrest issued, which may be used in all proceedings to set aside such order, as if the original order and affidavit were produced.

R.S. 2:33–24. A warrant of arrest issued pursuant to the provisions of this chapter shall be a full and complete protection to any constable, jailer, warden or other officer or person taking or detaining the body of a defendant thereunder, against liability for any act done or committed pursuant to the commands of the warrant in or about such taking or detention prior to the service upon him of a copy of the order mentioned in section 2:33-30 of this title, certified by the justice with whom it has been filed.

R.S. 2:33–25. If defendant does not give a bond for his appearance, as provided by section 2:33-26 of this title, the constable serving the warrant shall forthwith take defendant before the justice who issued the same. 

The justice shall thereupon allow defendant to enter into recognizance as provided by section 2:33-28 of this title, or, if defendant does not enter into such recognizance, shall command the constable to take defendant to the county jail, to be there detained until the recognizance is given or until the trial of the cause, which shall be set down for trial at a time and place certain, on a day not exceeding three days from the return of the warrant of arrest, or the justice may direct the constable to hold defendant in custody until plaintiff is notified and have time to appear and proceed to the trial of the cause at once if defendant consents thereto. 

R.S. 2:33-26. A constable serving a warrant of arrest shall, at the time of making the arrest, permit defendant to enter into a bond to defendant, with sufficient surety, in the amount of the debt or damages and costs indorsed on the warrant, for his appearance on the day and hour mentioned in the bond, not more than eight days from the service of the warrant, which bond the constable shall deliver to the justice on the return of the warrant, who shall file the same in his office to and for the use of the plaintiff in the action. The bond shall be substantially in the following form:

We, A. B. and C. D., do hereby bind ourselves jointly and severally to E. F. in the sum of to be paid to E. F. on the following conditions: that if the said A. B. shall be and appear before the small cause court held by one of the justices of the peace of at o'clock on noon, the .. day of and upon such other day to which the hearing may be lawfully adjourned, and answer unto the complaint of

the said E. F., then this bond to be void, or else to be and remain in full force and virtue; in witness whereof we have hereunto set our hands and seals, the .... day of one thousand nine hundred and

If individual and not corporate surety is given, the surety shall consist of at least one person residing in the county and having sufficient freehold. 

R.S. 2:33-27. When defendant has given a bond pursuant to section 2:33-26 of this title, the constable who served the warrant shall attend upon the small cause court on the day and hour mentioned in the bond, ready to take defendant into his custody. If the justice is not in attendance at the usual place of holding court at the time fixed therefor, defendant and his surety shall be permitted to consent in writing that the bond shall stand for his appearance at such future time, not exceeding ten days thereafter, as shall be then indorsed on the bond by the constable. 

R.S. 2:33-28. If defendant does not give a bond as provided by section 2:33-26 of this title, he may, when taken before the justice or at any time before the trial, enter into a recognizance to plaintiff, with sufficient surety, in the amount of the demand specified in the warrant, which recognizance the justice shall take and file in his office, for the benefit of the plaintiff in the action. The recognizance shall be substantially in the following form: 

Whereas, A. B. hath been arrested and is now in custody, by virtue of a warrant issued by C. D., one of the justices of the peace in and for the said county, at the suit of E. F., in an action for the sum of ; now be it remembered, that on the one thousand nine hundred and ....9 day of the said

A. B. and G. H. of the county aforesaid, personally appeared before me the said C. D., and jointly and severally acknowledged themselves to owe the said E. F. the

sum of to be made and levied of their several goods and chattels, upon condition that the recognizance shall be void, if the said A. B. shall appear on the day of next, before the said justice, or if he shall pay any judgment that may be recovered against him in said action, or if he shall surrender himself to the constable in the execution to be thereafter issued against him on such judgment, or if the said G. H. shall pay the said judgment for him; acknowledged the day and year last abovesaid, before me, C. D., one of the justices of the peace in and for the said county of If individual and not corporate surety is given, the surety shall consist of at least one person residing in the county and having sufficient freehold. 

R.S. 2:33–29. A defendant arrested upon a warrant may, at any time before the trial of the cause, apply to a justice of the supreme court, to a judge of the circuit court or court of common pleas or to a supreme court commissioner to have the order for arrest set aside. Reasonable notice of the application shall be given to plaintiff in the cause unless the supreme court justice, judge or commissioner shall elect to act without notice given. 

Giving bond or entering into a recognizance by defendant as herein provided shall not be a waiver of defendant's right to make such application. 

R.S. 2:33-30. If the supreme court justice, judge or commissioner deems the proof made insufficient to warrant an arrest, he may make an order directing the setting aside of the order therefor. The order so made, when filed with the justice of the peace, shall operate as a discontinuance of the action in which the warrant of arrest issued. The bond or recognizance given by defendant, if any, shall thereby become void; and plaintiff in the action shall thereupon be forever barred from proceeding against the body of the defendant for the

same cause of action, but may proceed by summons against the defendant as if no other proceeding had been instituted. The justice of the peace shall furnish certified copies of the order setting aside the arrest to the parties to the cause or their agents, upon request therefor. 

R.S. 2:33-31. Where a justice has made an order pursuant to section 2:33-22 of this title, and application has been made in the first instance to a justice of the supreme court to set the same aside and the justice of the supreme court has refused to do so, defendant shall not be permitted to renew his application. If the application has been made in the first instance to a judge of the circuit court or court of common pleas or a supreme court commissioner, who either grants or refuses the application, either party may, upon notice to the other party and within six days after such grant or refusal, apply to a justice of the supreme court, to review the action of the judge or commissioner, and he may, in his discretion, modify or set aside the order of the judge or commissioner, and make such other order in reference to the defendant's discharge from arrest on the warrant as he shall deem just or necessary. 

R.S. 2:33-32. If the recognizance provided for by section 2:33-28 of this title shall become forfeited the justice may, on five days' notice to the surety, to be served as a summons is served, enter judgment against the surety in favor of plaintiff for the amount of the judgment against defendant in the original action, with interest and costs, and issue execution thereon as in other cases. 

R.S. 2:33-33. Nothing herein contained shall be construed to prevent a constable from discharging from arrest any person detained on mesne process or process of execution, pursuant to subtitle 7 of this title (R.S. 2:34-1 

et seq.).

FORMS OF ACTION AND PLEADING 

R.S. 2:33-34. All actions in a small cause court shall be styled actions at law. 

R.S. 2:33-35. Plaintiff in an action or proceeding in a small cause court shall, on or before the return of the summons or warrant, file with the justice his state of demand against the defendant. In default thereof, plaintiff shall be non-suited, with cost. 

R.S. 2:33-36. If defendant have any demand against plaintiff, he shall, on or before the first trial day or the day to which the trial shall be first adjourned, file the same as a set-off or recoupment against plaintiff's demand. In default thereof defendant's demand shall not be received in evidence on the trial of the cause; and defendant shall forever thereafter be barred from maintaining any action for such demand or from setting off the same in any future action, except where the balance found to be due to defendant exceeds the sum or value of two hundred dollars and such excess has not been waived. 

R.S. 2:33-37. If plaintiff's state of demand has been served upon defendant pursuant to section 2:33-19 of this title, defendant shall not be entitled to an adjournment on the return day, except upon reasonable cause shown by affidavit. 

R.S. 2:33-38. Defendant, in an action on a contract, whether under seal or not, may set up, as a defense in abatement of the debt or damages sought to be recovered by plaintiff, a defect in or partial failure of the consideration of the contract sued on. He may also recoup any damages he may have sustained by reason of the nonperformance or defective performance of any part of the same contract by plaintiff.

When any such defense or counterclaim is set up, defendant shall file with the justice, on the day required 

[Honeyman's Justice Supp.]-2

for filing a set-off, a bill of particulars of such defense or counterclaim; and, if defendant recoups damages and the amount thereof is found to exceed the amount of plaintiff's demand, judgment shall be given for defendant against plaintiff in the action for such excess, with costs. 

R.S. 2:33-39. When, in an action in a small cause court, a defendant pleads as a justification title to real estate in himself or another under whom he has acted or entered, he shall make such plea in writing and sign and deliver it to the justice, who shall countersign and deliver it to plaintiff, and thereupon dismiss the action. 

If plaintiff shall, within sixty days thereafter, commence an action for the same cause in the supreme court or in the circuit court of the proper county, defendant in such action shall be entitled to plead as a justification no other defense than the title to the real estate in himself or another under whom he acted or entered; and, if the plaintiff recovers any damages in the action in the supreme or circuit court, he shall recover his costs. 

R.S. 2:33-40. The justice, to whom a plea of justification is tendered, shall, before receiving the same, take from the defendant a bond, with sufficient surety, in the penalty of one hundred dollars, executed to plaintiff in the action, and conditioned that, if plaintiff shall commence his action in the supreme or circuit court pursuant to section 2:33-39 of this title, defendant will appear thereto and pay such costs as may be awarded against him therein. If individual and not corporate surety is given, the surety shall consist of at least one person residing in the county and having sufficient freehold. If defendant does not give the bond herein required, the justice shall proceed in the action as if the plea had not been tendered. 

[Honeyman's Justice Supp.]

REFERENCES 

R.S. 2:33-41. In every action commenced in a small cause court, the justice shall, with the assent and at the request of the parties, enter a rule of reference of the matters in dispute to such person or persons as shall be named and agreed upon by the parties. 

R.S. 2:33-42. A reference made pursuant to the provisions of section 2:33-41 of this title shall be conducted in the manner prescribed by sections 2:27-178 to 2:27183 of this title. 

R.S. 2:33-43. The report of the referee or referees, or the majority of them, whether in favor of the plaintiff or defendant, shall be final and conclusive between the parties, and judgment shall be entered thereon, with costs, and execution issued accordingly.

DEPOSITIONS 

R.S. 2:33-44. The deposition of a material witness within the state, but ill or infirm or about to leave the state, may be taken for use in an action in a small cause court, at the option of either party, before any justice of the peace. Two days' notice, or such shorter notice as the justice before whom the action is pending shall deem proper, of the time and place of and the person before whom the deposition is to be taken, shall be given to the adverse party by the party at whose instance the deposition is to be taken, to attend at the taking thereof, and cross-examine, if he shall set fit. 

R.S. 2:33-45. A person whose deposition is taken pursuant to section 2:33-44 of this title shall be sworn, his testimony shall be reduced to writing only by the justice taking the deposition or the deponent or other person in his presence, and shall be then subscribed by the deponent.

R.S. 2:33–46. When a deposition has been taken as herein provided, the justice taking it shall retain it until he delivers it, with his own hand, into the court for which it was taken, or it shall be by him, sealed up, directed and transmitted to such court, to there remain under his seal until opened in such court. When so opened it shall be deposited in the office of the justice before whom the action is pending, there to remain on record. Either of the parties to the action may, at his own cost, take copies of the deposition as soon as it has been deposited. 

R.S. 2:33-47. A deposition taken as herein provided and offered in evidence shall be subject to the same rules and exceptions as would be the testimony of the witness if taken in open court.

TRIAL 

R.S. 2:33-48. Except where defendant consents to an immediate trial on the return of the warrant, the day mentioned in the summons, or in the bond or recognizance, for the appearance of defendant, or, where no bond or recognizance is given, the day set by the justice shall be the first trial day of the action, and the cause shall be tried on that day, at the time and place specified, unless an adjournment is granted. 

R.S. 2:33–49. If defendant does not appear at said time and place on the first trial day, or at the time and place to which the cause is adjourned, without sufficient reason being given for his nonappearance, the justice may hear the cause in his absence.

If plaintiff does not so appear, without sufficient reason being given for his nonappearance, the justice shall grant defendant a nonsuit. If defendant is entitled to move the trial on a demand filed against plaintiff, the justice may hear the cause in plaintiff's absence.

R.S. 2:33-50. In order to prevent fraud or surprise, or for other reasonable cause, the justice may adjourn the trial of a cause, at the request of either party, to a day not exceeding thirty days from the return day of the summons, or from the time when the warrant was returned, or from the time mentioned in the bond or recognizance for defendant's appearance.

If defendant is in jail under a warrant, no adjournment shall be granted on plaintiff's application, but one adjournment of not to exceed five days shall be granted to defendant, upon his request therefor. 

R.S. 2:33-51. If the application for an adjournment is made on the ground that the applicant cannot go to trial safely for want of a material witness being out of the state, who shall be named in the application, and such fact is established by affidavit, the justice may, subject to the restriction contained in the second paragraph of section 2:33-50 of this title, adjourn the trial to a time not exceeding three months. 

R.S. 2:33-52. All adjournments shall be made to an hour between nine A. M. and three P. M., both inclusive, unless the parties mutually agree upon another hour. 

R.S. 2:33-53. If defendant's demand against plaintiff is not filed until the first adjourned day, plaintiff, upon request therefor, shall be entitled to an adjournment for that cause for not to exceed three days. 

R.S. 2:33-54. Plaintiff, when a demand has been filed against him by defendant, shall not withdraw his action, nor shall any judgment of nonsuit or discontinuance be entered without defendant's consent, but the case shall be heard on defendant's motion, if plaintiff neglects or refuses to move the same. If it shall appear, upon evidence produced by defendant, that plaintiff is actually

indebted to defendant, judgment shall be rendered for defendant for the amount found due him.

The procedure above indicated shall be had only when defendant shall have filed an affidavit with the justice at the time of filing his demand, that the demand is not filed for the purpose of delay, and that, he verily believes that he does not owe the plaintiff anything, or that he owes only a certain sum, stating such sum, and that plaintiff is indebted to him in a certain sum, stating such sum. 

R.S. 2:33-55. Either party to an action in a small cause court may, after defendant has appeared and before the justice has begun to inquire into the merits of the cause, demand a trial by jury, which the justice shall thereupon grant. 

R.S. 2:33–56. Upon demand for a trial by jury as provided by section 2:33–55 of this title, a venire shall issue to summon a jury of six persons, without regard to sex, if the demand or matter in dispute does not exceed fifty dollars, or a like jury of twelve persons, if the demand or matter in dispute exceeds fifty dollars. 

Jurors so summoned shall be citizens of this state, above the age of twenty-one years and under the age of sixty-five years, and shall in nowise be akin to the plaintiff or defendant nor interested in the action. 

R.S. 2:33-57. The venire shall require the jurors therein summoned to appear before the justice at such time and place as shall be designated therein, to make a jury for the trial of the action between the parties mentioned therein. 

R.S. 2:33–58. The constable shall, at the return of the venire, return, annexed thereto, a panel containing the names of the jurors summoned by virtue of the venire. 

R.S. 2:33-59. If, upon the return of the venire, it appears that a juror summoned is disqualified to serve or

does not appear, the constable who served the venire shall, by order of the justice, immediately summon another to serve in his stead. 

R.S. 2:33-60. If a jury summoned as herein provided shall disagree, other writs of venire may issue in the cause, until a verdict is obtained. 

R.S. 2:33-61. To each juror summoned as provided in this chapter the justice shall administer the following oath: "You do swear in the presence of Almighty God (or affirm), that you will well and truly try the matter in difference between plaintiff, and defendant, and a true verdict give, acording to the evidence." 

R.S. 2:33-62. To the constable appointed to attend a jury, the justice shall administer the following oath: "You do swear in the presence of Almighty God (or affirm), that you will, to the utmost of your ability, keep every person sworn (or affirmed) on this jury together in some private and convenient place without meat or drink, water excepted, that you will not suffer any person to speak to them nor speak to them yourself, except by order of the court, unless it be to ask them whether they have agreed on their verdict, until they have agreed on their verdict." 

R.S. 2:33-63. When plaintiff, in an action on contract, demands a jury of twelve, and the jury finds for the plaintiff in an amount not exceeding fifty dollars, he shall pay one-half of the jury costs.

R.S. 2:33-64. Every person summoned as a juror or subpoenaed as a witness, not appearing, or appearing and refusing to serve or give evidence in an action in a small cause court without reasonable cause being shown, shall, for every such default or refusal, be liable to a fine

of not more than twenty dollars nor less than one dollar, as the justice shall think proper to impose. 

A justice imposing a fine as herein authorized shall, unless the same be paid, issue an execution, directed to any constable of the county, commanding him to levy the amount of the fine out of the goods and chattels of the offender.

All fines paid or collected as herein provided shall be paid over by the justice to the county treasurer for the use of the county. 

R.S. 2:33-65. To each witness produced at the trial of a cause in a small cause court the justice shall administer the following oath: "You do swear in the presence of Almighty God (or affirm), that the evidence you shall give in this matter in difference between plaintiff, and defendant, shall be the truth, the whole truth, and nothing but the truth." 

R.S. 2:33-66. When the nature of plaintiff's demand is such that his books of account are requisite to the proof of his cause of action, a copy of the entries therein, in so far as they relate to plaintiff's demand, together with a statement of the credits or allowances, if any, to which defendant is entitled, shall, if defendant does not appear at the trial or if appearing, does not require the production of the books of original entry, be received in evidence with the same effect as the books themselves. 

The copy of the entries shall be accompanied by an affidavit stating that the copy is a true copy of the original entries and either that all credits and allowances to which defendant is entitled appear in the statement thereof or that defendant is not entitled to any credits or allowances, and that the amount or balance claimed by plaintiff is justly due and owing to him. 

A partnership may be proved in like case and manner.

R.S. 2:33-67. If, in an action on a promissory note, bill of exchange, check, draft or other written contract, simple or under seal, for the payment of money only, defendant does not appear at the hearing of the cause, or if he appears and consents thereto, plaintiff may prove his case by affidavit, which shall contain a copy of the instrument sued on and set forth and aver such facts and circumstances as would warrant a recovery if proved by witnesses. 

R.S. 2:33-68. In an action on a promissory note, bill of exchange, check, draft or other written contract, whether simple or under seal, for the payment of money only, where proof is made by affidavit as provided in section 2:33-67 of this title, the instrument sued on must be produced at the hearing of the cause, or its nonproduction accounted for by affidavit. 

R.S. 2:33-69. When proof is made by affidavit it must appear therefrom that affiant has competent knowledge of the facts sworn to. 

R.S. 2:33-70. In so far as applicable, the provisions of subtitle 11 of this title (R.S. 2:97-1 et seq.) shall extend to the trial of actions in small cause courts.

JUDGMENT AND EXECUTION 

R.S. 2:33-71. If plaintiff in an action in a small cause court other than an executor or administrator, is nonsuited, or discontinues or withdraws his action without the consent of defendant, where he may lawfully do so, judgment shall be given against him for the accrued costs. 

R.S. 2:33-72. A judgment by confession entered against defendant in an action in a small cause court shall be binding against and effective as to the parties to the action only, unless an affidavit is first made as

provided by section 2:27-275 of this title, which affidavit shall be filed by the justice.

R.S. 2:33-73. After a cause has been submitted to a jury upon the merits by either party no judgment of nonsuit or of discontinuance shall be entered, except by consent of the parties. 

R.S. 2:33-74. When judgment shall be given and entered against a plaintiff or defendant in an action in a small cause court, the justice shall grant execution thereon, commanding the constable to levy and make the debt, or damages and costs, of the goods and chattels of the party, and, in the cases hereinafter specified, for want of sufficient goods and chattels whereon to levy and make the same, to take the body of such party and convey him to the jail of the county; but no execution shall issue against the body of a female. 

R.S. 2:33-75. Subject to the prohibition contained in section 2:33-74 of this title, an execution against the body shall issue in the following cases only: a. Where a warrant has been issued pursuant to sections 2:33-21 and 2:33-22 of this title and remains in force; b. Where no warrant has been issued and, the action being founded upon contract, express or implied, proof is made by affidavit to the satisfaction of the justice, establishing the existence of the conditions mentioned in section 2:33-21 of this title; c. Where no warrant has been issued and, the action being founded upon contract, express or implied, or upon a tort, proof is made by affidavit to the satisfaction of the justice establishing that defendant has property or rights which he conceals, or rights or credits, moneys or effects, in his own possession or in the possession of another to his use, of the value of fifty dollars or over in

excess of exempt property, which he refuses to apply in payment of the judgment against him; d. Where the judgment is given in an action founded upon a tort and proof is made by affidavit to the satisfaction of the justice that defendant is not a freeholder in the county in which the court is held. 

No execution shall issue against the body upon a judgment entered in an action to recover damages for the conversion of property where defendant, as disclosed by the pleadings and evidence, obtained possession of the property lawfully, under a contract for the conditional sale thereof, or under a lease or other contract whereby the title to such property was to vest in him only after a certain sum of money had been paid thereon. 

R.S. 2:33-76. Upon proof made pursuant to section 

2:33-75 of this title, the justice shall file the proof and make and subscribe an order that execution issue against the goods and chattels of defendant, and, for want of sufficient goods and chattels, against the body, and the execution may issue in accordance with the order. 

R.S. 2:33-77. The justice making an order pursuant to section 2:33-76 of this title, shall furnish to defendant or his agent, upon request therefor, a certified copy of the order or affidavit upon which the warrant or execution against the body issued, which may be used in all proceedings to set aside such order, as if the original order and affidavit were produced. 

R.S. 2:33-78. Except where a previous application to set aside an order for a warrant of arrest has been made and passed upon pursuant to sections 2:33-29 and 2:3330 of this title, a defendant may, at any time after an order has been made pursuant to section 2:33-76 of this title, apply as provided by section 2:33-29 and 2:33-30 to have the order for execution set aside, in so far as it

authorizes the taking of the body. Reasonable notice of the application shall first be given to plaintiff. If the justice of the supreme court, judge of the circuit court or court of common pleas or supreme court commissioner finds the proofs made insufficient to warrant the issue of execution against the body, he may order that the order of the justice of the peace, in so far as it authorizes the taking of defendant's body, be set aside. 

R.S. 2:33-79. The order to set aside the body execution shall be delivered to the justice, who shall file it with the other papers in the cause and furnish certified copies to the defendant or his agent on request therefor. The order to set aside shall, from the time of its filing with the justice, operate as a discharge of defendant from arrest or imprisonment and from liability thereto. Upon the service of a certified copy of the order to set aside the body execution upon the person in whose custody the defendant may be, he shall immediately discharge defendant from arrest or imprisonment under the execution. 

R.S. 2:33-80. When a justice has made an order pursuant to section 2:33-76 of this title, and application has been made in the first instance to a justice of the supreme court, to set aside the order and the supreme court justice has refused to do so, defendant shall not be permitted to renew his application. If the application has been made to a judge of the circuit court or court of common pleas or a supreme court commissioner, who either allows or refuses the application, either party may, within six days after the order has been allowed or refused and on notice to the other party, apply to a justice of the supreme court, to review the action of the judge or commissioner, and the justice of the supreme court may, in his discretion, modify or set aside the order of the judge or commissioner, and make such order in reference to the discharge or taking of defendant's body, on any execu-

tion issued or that may be issued as the nature of the case may justify or require. 

R.S. 2:33-81. When execution is issued against the body as provided by this chapter, the constable to whom the writ is directed shall, for want of goods and chattels whereon to levy, according to the tenor thereof, take the body of the person against whom the execution is issued and convey and deliver him to the sheriff of the county, who shall keep the person to him delivered in safe custody until the debt or damages is fully paid, with costs, or until he is delivered thence according to law. 

The constable shall take the jailer's receipt upon the execution and return the same to the justice who issued it, who shall make a record thereof in his docket. 

R.S. 2:33-82. If the keeper of the jail, to whom the body of person taken on execution has been delivered pursuant to section 2:33-81 of this title, suffers the person so committed to his custody to go or be at large out of the jail before the debt or damages with costs, be paid, or he be thence delivered by due course of law, such sufference shall be deemed to be an escape, for which the sheriff shall be responsible to the plaintiff in the cause to the amount of the debt or damages and costs, for which such person shall be committed, recoverable by plaintiff, in an action at law, with costs.

This section shall not apply to cases where the person in custody has been taken therefrom by a writ of habeas corpus or has been discharged pursuant to an order made pursuant to section 2:33-78 to 2:33-80 of this title. 

R.S. 2:33-83. A writ of execution against the body, issued pursuant to the provisions of this chapter, shall be a full and complete protection to any constable, jailer, warden or other officer or person taking or detaining the body of a defendant thereunder, against liability for any

act done or committed pursuant to the commands of the writ in or about such taking or detention prior to the service upon him of a copy of the order mentioned in section 2:33–78 and 2:33–79 of this title, certified by the justice with whom it has been filed. 

R.S. 2:33-84. When, in any action, defendant shall have judgment against plaintiff for debt or damages, he shall have against plaintiff the same execution as he would be entitled to if he had been plaintiff in the action, enforceable in the same manner and with the same limitations as if it had been issued in favor of a plaintiff. 

R.S. 2:33-85. If a defendant, at any time before the issue of an execution against him as herein provided, procures a good and sufficient freeholder, of and resident of the county, to join with him in a confession of judgment to the adverse party for the amount of his demand, with costs, no execution shall issue, if the judgment is not for more than fifty dollars, until after one month from the time of the entry thereof or, if for more than fifty dollars, until after three months from the time of the entry thereof. This section shall not apply to an action upon a judgment recovered in a small cause court. 

R.S. 2:33-86. An execution issued pursuant to the provisions of this chapter shall be operative and effective against the goods and chattels levied on for one year from the date of the issue thereof, unless sooner satisfied. An execution remaining unsatisfied for one year from the date of its issue shall be null and void. 

R.S. 2:33-87. Upon the return of an execution unsatisfied, the justice issuing it may issue other executions from time to time and within six years, without notice to defendant, which may be levied on the goods and chattels of defendant, made returnable and executed as the original execution.

R.S. 2:33-88. An execution may issue without a revival by scire facias of the judgment upon which it is founded at any time within twenty years from the recovery, if the justice, by successive elections or otherwise, continues so long in office. If more than six years has elapsed since the recovery of the judgment, a special order of the justice shall be necessary before the execution can issue. 

R.S. 2:33-89. Five days' notice of the application for the special order mentioned in section 2:33-88 of this title shall be given to defendant and proof made to the satisfaction of the justice of the nonpayment of or the amount remaining due on the judgment. The notice may be served upon defendant personally, either within or without the state, or, if he resides within the state, by leaving a copy thereof at his usual place of abode with a person of his family over fourteen years of age, who shall be informed of its contents. Proof of the notice and of the time and manner of its service shall be made and filed with the justice. 

R.S. 2:33-90. When execution has issued upon a judgment entered in a small cause court and has been returned unsatisfied in whole or in part and defendant, against whom the execution has been issued and returned resides or has goods and chattels in a county other than the one in which the execution was issued and returned, an alias or pluries execution, as the case may be, may issue out of the court in which the judgment was entered. 

R.S. 2:33-91. The alias or pluries execution mentioned in section 2:33-90 of this title shall be directed to a constable of the county wherein defendant resides or may be found or in which he has goods and chattels. The constable, to whom the alias or pluries execution has been delivered, shall present the same to a justice of the peace of the county wherein the defendant resides, may

be found or has goods or chattels, who shall, on being satisfied by proof of the signature of the justice issuing it, indorse his name thereon, with an authority to make levy and sale of the goods and chattels of defendant in the same manner as other executions issuing out of a small cause court are levied and sales had. The execution shall be returned to the justice issuing it as in other cases. 

R.S. 2:33-92. If one or more executions issued pursuant to the provisions of this chapter are levied by one or more constables upon the goods and chattels of a defendant, they shall have priority as of the time of the levy. All surplus moneys from a sale under an execution shall be paid to the officer or person holding the execution next in priority, and so on in the line of priority, until all executions levied upon the goods and chattels of a defendant at the time of sale under the first execution, are satisfied in whole or in part out of such surplus, according to their respective priorities. 

If two or more executions in favor of different plaintiffs are levied at the same time, they shall have preference as of the time when they were received by the constable for levy, which date shall be noted on each execution by the constable at the time he receives the same. 

If two or more executions against the same defendant are delivered to a constable at the same time, the moneys from a sale under such executions, or either of them, shall, if insufficient to satisfy both or all of them, be applied to their satisfaction in proportion to the amounts due thereon respectively. 

R.S. 2:33-93. The constable to whom an execution is delivered shall make an inventory in writing of such of the property of defendant as he intends to levy upon. 

The inventory and levy and the actual time of making the same shall be annexed to the execution and signed by the constable, and shall, at all times, be received as evidence

of the levy and of the time of its making. The property levied upon shall be bound from the time of the levy, and not before. 

R.S. 2:33-94. Every constable levying on goods and chattels under an execution shall give at least five days' notice of the time and place they will be exposed to sale, by advertisements, signed by himself and posted in three of the most conspicuous places in the municipality where the goods and chattels were taken. He shall therein describe the goods and chattels to be sold. At the time and place appointed he shall expose the goods and chattles taken to public sale, strike them off to the highest bidder, pay the proceeds of the sale to plaintiff, or to the justice in the absence of plaintiff, and, within thirty days from the time he received the execution, make return to the justice of the proceedings had thereon, who shall make a record thereof.

R.S. 2:33-95. When a written claim to goods and chattels levied on or taken under an execution is made to the constable in possession thereof by a person other than defendant, the constable shall immediately delay the sale thereunder for ten days, to permit the claimant to apply, within such time, to a justice of the peace in or near the municipality wherein the goods and chattels were taken for a jury to try and determine his claim. 

If plaintiff in execution indemnifies the constable against claimant's demand, the proceeding to try the claim shall be suspended, and the constable shall proceed with the execution as therein directed.

If the claimant shall not, within ten days, apply to a justice and have his right tried as aforesaid, his claim shall be deemed to be abandoned, and the constable shall proceed as if no claim had been made. 

R.S. 2:33-96. The justice to whom the application 

[Honeyman's Justice Supp.]-3

mentioned in section 2:33-95 of this title has been made shall issue a venire for a jury of six qualified jurors, direct a return thereof to be made to him and proceed therein as in other cases of trial by jury. The claimant shall give notice in writing to the plaintiff in execution of the time and place of the trial of his claim. 

R.S. 2:33-97. If the jury finds that the goods and chattels belong to the claimant, the constable shall proceed no further with the execution. If it is found that they belong to defenadnt, the constable shall proceed with the execution as directed therein. 

The verdict of the jury shall protect the constable from liability for any action for taking and seizing the goods and chattels or delivering them to the claimant. 

R.S. 2:33-98. The costs of the trial of a claim to property levied upon shall be taxed by the justice as in other cases, and shall be paid by plaintiff in execution if the verdict of the jury is for the claimant, and by the claimant if the verdict is against him. 

R.S. 2:33-99. A justice, whose term of office has expired or who has resigned, shall, when requested so to do and if his docket is lawfully in his possession, issue final process of execution upon any judgment remaining unsatisfied upon his docket. He shall also, upon request, issue writs of scire facias to revive any judgment so remaining unsatisfied. The process and writs issued under authority of this section shall be returnable before such justice, and shall have the same force and effect and be liable to the same objections as though such justice were still in commission. 

R.S. 2:33-100. If a constable to whom an execution is delivered does not perform the duties, or any of them, prescribed by this chapter in relation thereto, he shall 

[Honeyman's Justice Supp.]

be liable to the person in whose favor the execution issued in the amount of the damages and costs mentioned therein, recoverable by the injured person in an action at law, with double costs. If a constable has in his hands more than one execution and has not performed the duties required of him by law in relation thereto, he shall, in like manner, be liable, separately or jointly, to the injured person or persons.

If a constable has received the money or any part thereof on an execution, he shall be liable, when sued therefor, to treble costs. 

R.S. 2:33-101. Final judgments of a small cause court, when not less than ten dollars, exclusive of costs, remain due thereon, may be docketed in the court of common pleas of the county in which the small cause court is held. 

R.S. 2:33-102. If a judgment is to be docketed under authority of section 2:33-101 of this title, there shall be filed with the clerk of the court of common pleas of the proper county a statement, signed by the justice before whom the judgment was recovered, under his hand and seal, containing the name of the justice, the names of the parties for and against whom the judgment was rendered, the amount and date of the judgment, and the date of issue and return of the execution issued thereon, if any. 

A justice of the peace whose term of office has expired or who has resigned may make and certify the statement with the same force and effect as if still in commission. 

There shall also be filed with the statement an affidavit of the plaintiff or his attorney, setting forth that the judgment about to be docketed is still due and unpaid, in whole or in part, stating the amount due. 

R.S. 2:33-103. When, from the docket of a justice which has been deposited in the office of the clerk of the court of common pleas pursuant to the provisions of section 2:33-150 of this title, it appears that there is en-

tered therein the record of a cause tried before such justice, and that a judgment has been entered in such cause for an amount not less than ten dollars, exclusive of costs, the clerk shall, upon the request of the person in whose favor the judgment was entered, his attorney, executors, administrators or assigns, and whether or not execution has been issued thereon, make and certify, under his hand and seal, a statement of the judgment, to the same effect as the statement required by section 2:33–102 of this title.

The statement so made and certified, when accompanied by plaintiff's affidavit as required by said section 

2:33-102 shall, for the purpose of docketing such judgment in the court of common pleas, be as effective as the justice's statement required by said section 2:33-102. 

R.S. 2:33-104. The clerk of each court of common pleas shall provide and keep a docket, in which shall be entered a copy of the statement provided for by either section 2:33-102 or section 2:33-103 of this title, together with a note of the amount due on the judgment as shown by plaintiff's affidavit.

Each entry so made shall have the same force and effect as a judgment of the court of common pleas. 

R.S. 2:33–105. Each clerk of a court of common pleas shall make and keep a complete alphabetical index to the docket mentioned in section 2:33-104 of this title. 

The dockets and indexes thereto shall be public records, to which all persons shall have access. 

R.S. 2:33-106. After a judgment of a small cause court has been docketed in a court of common pleas as herein provided, no execution shall issue thereon out of the small cause court, nor shall any proceedings be had thereon except on appeal or certiorari. 

R.S. 2:33–107. A judgment docketed in a court of

common pleas as herein directed may be revived by scire facias in the court of common pleas as original judgments of a court of common pleas may be revived. 

APPEALS 

R.S. 2:33–108. Either party to an action in a small cause court may appeal from a judgment obtained therein, except a judgment given by confession, to the court of common pleas of the county in which the small cause court was held, by filing a notice of appeal with the justice, within twenty days after the judgment is given. 

The justice shall thereupon grant the appeal as hereinafter directed.

R.S. 2:33-109. The appeal mentioned in section 2:33108 of this title may be granted with effect by the justice, although his term of office has expired or he has resigned. 

R.S. 2:33-110. No appeal from the judgment of a justice shall be allowed until appellant has paid to the justice all costs accruing to the justice, except such as may be adjudged to the prevailing party. 

R.S. 2:33–111. The appeal shall be taken by notice in writing, signed by or in behalf of appellant, briefly describing the judgment appealed from and stating that appellant appeals therefrom to the court of common pleas. 

R.S. 2:33-112. One of several defendants may appeal from a judgment against them collectively, without a summons or order of severance or leave to prosecute the appeal alone from the appellate court, upon filing the notice required by section 2:33-108 and the bond required by section 2:33-113 of this title. 

R.S. 2:33-113. If the judgment appealed from is against appellant, or if, in the action in which the judgment was given, there is an offset against his demand,

appellant shall file with the justice a bond to appellee, with sufficient surety, in double the amount of the judgment or offset, and conditioned that appellant will appear and prosecute his appeal in the court of common pleas, and will abide the judgment of that court and pay the costs taxed against him if the judgment is affirmed. If individual and not corporate surety is given, the surety shall consist of at least one surety residing in the county and having sufficient freehold. If the judgment appealed from is in favor of appellant, and there is no offset against his demand, no appeal bond shall be required. 

R.S. 2:33–114. The court of common pleas may permit appellant to substitute a new appeal bond for the bond filed and set up by the justice. 

R.S. 2:33–115. The justice granting an appeal shall, within ten days after receiving notice of an appeal and under his hand and seal, send to the clerk of the court of common pleas to which the appeal is made a transcript of the proceedings and judgment in the cause, together with the bond filed with him.

R.S. 2:33–116. The fees of the court of common pleas for hearing an appeal, and of the clerk of the court for entering the appeal and filing the appeal bond and transcript, shall be paid in the first instance by appellant. If appellant does not pay such fees before the hearing of the appeal, the court shall, on application of the clerk, refuse to hear appellant, and appellee may, if he pays such fees, move the court to, and the court may, make such disposition of the appeal as if appellant had failed to appear and prosecute his appeal. 

R.S. 2:33-117. Upon the filing of an appeal bond with the clerk, or on proof that it has been filed with the justice, the court of common pleas or a judge thereof at chambers, may, on proof of the legality and sufficiency of

the bond order a stay of any execution which may have been issued upon the judgment appealed from, until further order, a rule to which effect shall be entered in the minutes of the appellate court. A copy of the order, certified by the clerk of the court, shall be served on the constable in whose hands the execution may be. 

R.S. 2:33-118. If a judgment is removed by appeal and, either before or after such removal, is docketed in a court of common pleas as provided by section 2:33–101 to 2:33-107 of this title, execution from the court in which the judgment is docketed shall if the necessary bond is given and perfected, be stayed and suspended in the manner provided by section 2:33-117 of this title until the final determination of the appeal. 

R.S. 2:33-119. Appeals to the court of common pleas shall be placed by appellant therein on the list for trial at the next term after the appeal is perfected, if sufficient time to notice the appeal for ten days remains between the perfecting of the appeal and the first day of such term. 

R.S. 2:33-120. Either party may notice an appeal for trial and place the same on the trial list. In default of such notice the court, unless good cause be shown, shall dismiss the appeal. 

R.S. 2:33-121. All appeals shall be heard and determined by the judge of the court of common pleas without a jury, unless a demand for a jury is made in writing, signed and filed with the clerk of the court of common pleas at least five days before the day noticed for the trial of the appeal. 

R.S. 2:33-122. Either party may bring on the hearing of an appeal at term time or in vacation, upon ten days' notice to the other party or his attorney.

R.S. 2:33-123. On appeal to the court of common pleas from a judgment of a small cause court, that court shall, with the assent and at the request of the parties, refer the matters in dispute to a referee or referees in the manner and with the effect set forth in sections 2:3341 to 2:33-43 of this title.

R.S. 2:33-124. The court of common pleas shall hear and determine all appeals summarily, and give judgment and award execution thereon with costs, either on the affirmance or reversal of the judgment below. 

R.S. 2:33–125. Upon the trial of an appeal either party may, without notice to the opposite party, produce witnesses not produced or sworn, or documentary evidence not offered or admitted, in the court below, if otherwise legal and competent. 

R.S. 2:33–126. If a judgment appealed from was rendered upon the report of referees, the appellate court may inquire into such report and the judgment thereupon, and either affirm or set aside the same for the same causes and upon the same principles as reports of referees are set aside in such court in other cases and award a trial before the court, if need be, in the same manner as in other cases. 

R.S. 2:33–127. After the trial of an appeal in a court of common pleas that court may grant a new trial. 

R.S. 2:33-128. No judgment of a court of common pleas on an appeal from a small cause court shall affect or bind any real estate, unless a rule be entered in the minutes of the court of common pleas in which the judgment is rendered for recording such judgment, which rule shall be a rule of course and may be entered at any time without notice. 

R.S. 2:33–129. The clerk of a court of common pleas,

upon the entry of a rule pursuant to section 2:33-128 of this title, shall record the judgment in the book of judgments of the court and index the same as original judgments of the court of common pleas are recorded and indexed, which record shall be a transcript from the minutes of the court of common pleas on the judgment. 

R.S. 2:33-130. A judgment of a court of common pleas on appeal from a small cause court shall, from the time of the entry of a rule pursuant to section 2:33–128 of this title, affect and bind all real estate in the county in which the court is held and belonging to the person against whom the judgment is rendered; and executions against the goods and real estate of such person may issue out of the court of common pleas immediately upon the entry of the rule. 

R.S. 2:33–131. If a judgment of a small cause court, docketed in a court of common pleas pursuant to sections 

2:33-101 to 2:33-107 of this title is reversed upon appeal to a court of common pleas and a duly certified transcript of the judgment of the court wherein such appeal may have been determined shall be delivered to the clerk of the court of common pleas, the clerk shall enter in the margin of the docket opposite the entry of the judgment the substance, in short form, of the determination upon the appeal. 

R.S. 2:33-132. On appeal to a court of common pleas from a judgment of a small cause court rendered upon the verdict of a jury, the court of common pleas may, before hearing and determining the appeal, make all amendments which it would have made had the cause been commenced originally before it. If such amendments shall not actually be made, because of inconvenience in the making thereof, everything so amendable shall be taken and considered as amended, and proceed-

ings shall be had thereupon as if the amendments had been actually made. 

CERTIORARI 

R.S. 2:33–133. Judgments, orders or proceedings had or made by virtue of this chapter, shall be removed into the supreme court or circuit court by certiorari, and by certiorari only. 

R.S. 2:33-134. All judgments, orders and proceedings in the small cause courts and in the courts of common pleas upon appeals from the small cause courts may be removed into the circuit court of the same county on writ of certiorari, allowed by any judge of such court, in the same manner and upon like terms as such writs are issuable out of the supreme court. 

R.S. 2:33-135. When a small cause court has jurisdiction, no judgment from which an appeal is given by this chapter shall be removed into the supreme court or circuit court by certiorari or otherwise for the correction of an alleged error therein. The party thinking himself aggrieved shall have relief by appeal only, both as to matters of law and of fact. 

R.S. 2:33–136. Before a writ of certiorari is granted or allowed, the applicant therefor shall: a. Present in writing to the justice or judge the reasons for his application, signed by the applicant or an attorney at law, which shall show sufficient probable cause, in the opinion of the justice or judge, for the allowance of the writ; and b. Enter into a bond to the adverse party, in double the amount of the judgment, with sufficient surety, and conditioned that the applicant will prosecute the writ in the court to which application is made, will pay the amount recovered in the court below, with interest and costs, if the judgment is affirmed, and will abide the

judgment of the supreme or circuit court respecting the judgment, order or proceeding of the court below. 

R.S. 2:33–137. The bond required by section 2:33–136 of this title shall be tendered to the justice or judge to whom the application for the writ is made, who shall file it with the clerk of his court for the benefit of the obligee named therein. No certiorari shall be allowed if the bond be not tendered and filed. 

R.S. 2:33-138. The certiorari shall be prosecuted before the supreme or circuit court at the first term after the due return thereof, or be dismissed with costs, unless, for good cause, the court shall otherwise direct. A motion to dismiss for a failure to prosecute the writ shall be a matter of course without notice thereof being required. 

R.S. 2:33–139. If any judgment recovered in a small cause court shall be removed by certiorari and either before or after such removal, shall be docketed in a court of common pleas, execution from the court in which the judgment is docketed shall, if the bond required by section 2:33-136 of this title is given and perfected, be stayed and suspended until the final determination of the certiorari. 

R.S. 2:33-140. On certiorari, the supreme court or circuit court may, if it appears equitable and just, order a rehearing of the cause or proceeding before the small cause court, upon such terms and conditions as are reasonable. The small cause court shall thereupon rehear the cause or proceeding and give judgment as in other cases. 

R.S. 2:33-141. A judgment of a small cause court removed by certiorari shall not be reversed in its entirety for: a. Error or mistake of the justice rendering the judg-

ment in entering the same or calculating the amount thereof or in awarding costs, but the judgment shall be corrected only as to any such error or mistake; or b. Irregularity in the proceedings in the small cause court, unless it tends to defeat or impair the substantial right or interest of the party in certiorari praying reversal; or c. Error by a constable in the return of a summons as to its service, if defendant was in fact duly and legally served. 

R.S. 2:33-142.
