§ 99. Judicial Notice. The court will take judicial notice of events and conditions which are matters of common knowledge, such as conditions of economic emergency.82 § 100. Presumptions. A presumption exists in an instance where a specific, logical, and reasonable inference from a particular fact or from particular evidence is considered as a fact until disproved by evidence or by a stronger presumption. To be allowable, a presumption of fact must be an immediate inference to be drawn from facts proved.84

The presumption is not evidence in itself but is merely a rule concerning evidence,85 the effect of which is to place on the opposing party the duty of going forward with the evidence; thus, it may be seen that the presumption may be the basis of a prima-facie case.86 83 The law always raises a presumption against wrongdoing.87 Collusion and fraud are matters which are not presumed but must be proved, the inference or presumption, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, being one of honesty. 88 There being no proof to the contrary, a presumption exists that every person possesses and enjoys a normal

§ 103. Res Gestae. A declaration which is concomi tant with the main fact under consideration and is so connected with it as to illustrate its character may be proved as part of the res gestae.97 However, if the dec-

Res gestae have been said to consist of statements or exclamations made by an injured person or by one present at the accident immediately after the injury declaring the circumstances thereof,98 The test for determining whether declarations made after the occurrence of an event are part of the res gestae lies in the answer to the question whether the speaker has had time to reflect and to make up his mind as to the happening and then give utterance to self-serving statements.98 "If the speaker has had such opportunity to reflect and make a statement helpful to himself, or if the statement is a narrative, after the happening and lacking in spontaneity, the statement is inadmissible."'98 

§ 104. Parol Evidence. The general rule on this mat ter is that parol evidence is not admissible to vary the terms of a written instrument:99 where the parties have deliberately put their engagements into writing in such terms as import legal obligation without any uncertainty as to the object or extent of the engagements, there arises a conclusive presumption to the effect that the whole contract and the extent and manner of the undertaking has been reduced to writing, and, therefore, all oral testimony is rejected.¹

This general rule applies only in those instances where the parties have assented to certain writing or writings as the statement of the contract between them; hence, the rule does not operate to preclude the admission of

The parol-evidence rule is applicable to negotiable instruments. A receipt is merely an evidence of payment and may be contradicted by oral evidence, but such is not the rule in the case of a release."

This rule as to the inadmissibility of parol evidence is not applicable in the case where a third party is attacking the written instrument. § 105. Judgments. § 111. Body Executions. A defendant, seeking to be discharged from a body execution, may appear before the court at any time during the term after the term at which he has given bond.15 He may not appear during the term he has entered the bond." § 112. Income. Should more than one execution issue from a court or courts of this State against the wages, debts, earnings, salary, trust income, or profits due and owing to the same judgment debtor, the executions are to be satisfied in the order of priority in which they are presented to the persons from whom such moneys are due and owing, including any county or the State.16 

Only one of the executions may be satisfied at one time, irrespective of any other law or laws of this State and irrespective of the fact that the executions have been issued from different courts.16 In no case may the amount specified in such a wage execution issued from any court exceed ten per centum, unless the debtor's income exceeds the sum of twenty-five hundred dollars in which instance the judge of the court whence the execution has issued may order a larger percentage."7

§ 118. Actions. An executor or administrator may bring an action for any trespass to the decedent's person or to his realty or personalty and may recover the damages as the decedent would if he were living.30 

A foreign executor or administrator may prosecute an action or obtain an execution on a judgment or decree in any court of this State or may be made a party defendant to any action at law or suit in equity in any court of this State, as though his letters were granted in this State, providing that there be filed in the office of the register of the Prerogative Court an exemplified copy of his letters testamentary or of administration.31 Security for the costs may be required from such representative as though he were a non-resident.81 Such actions at law or suits in equity by or against the foreign representative are valid, providing that prior to, pending, or subsequent to, the action or suit, the exemplified copy of the letters is so filed.31

Attorney's fees for trial or argument

Court's fees for hearing appeal or argument 

Court's fees for signing judgment 

Clerk's fees for filing appeal papers and proceedings 

Filing notice of appeal before notice of hearing or trial

Filing notice of trial 

Order dismissing appeal

§ 121a. Lawyers' Fees. The Legislature enacted a law in 1939 to the effect that no fee or allowance by way of fee may be made to any attorney or counsel in any cause, matter, or proceeding in any court in this State, unless it be made for, or on account of, actual services rendered by a member of the Bar of this State engaged in the practice of law and maintaining an office in this State.342

XXXI. FERTILIZERS Section 122. Violation of Act. 

§ 122. Violation of Act. A violation of the 1938 Act as to fertilizers and other preparations for the soil entails a penalty of one hundred dollars for the first offense and of not less than one hundred dollars and not more than one thousand dollars for each subsequent offense, to be recovered in an action in the name of the director of the state agricultural experiment station.95

Should the judgment be rendered for the plaintiff, the court is to cause the defendant who fails to pay the amount of the judgment forthwith, as well as the costs and charges incident thereto, to be committed to the county jail for a period of not more than one hundred days.35 In case of a subsequent conviction and failure to pay the amount due, such period of imprisonment may be for any number of days not exceeding two hundred.35

Whether the small cause court or magistrate has jurisdiction over these actions is somewhat doubtful. The procedure is outlined in the statutes.36 

35. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 142, R.S. 4:9-13. 

36. 1 Rev. 1937, R.S. 4:23-1. 134

§ 123. Prohibition of Sale, Etc. One violating the 

Fireworks Act of 1937 is subject to the following penalties:87 for selling, offering or exposing for sale, or having in one's possession with the intent to sell any fireworks, the sum of one hundred dollars for each offense or imprisonment in the county jail for ninety days or both in the discretion of the court possessing jurisdiction, and for using, discharging, or causing to be discharged, ignited, fired, or otherwise set in action, or having in one's possession any fireworks, a sum not exceeding one hundred dollars for each offense.3 37 The municipalities of the State are charged with the enforcement of this Act.38

129a. Coyotes. 

129b. Procedure. § 124. Licenses. No person may at any time hunt, take, kill, or pursue, with a gun or any type of firearm, a wild bird, animal, or fowl or take or attempt to take any skunk, mink, muskrat, or other fur-bearing animal by means of a trap or set a trap for any fur-bearing animal and no male person above the age of fourteen years may at any time take or attempt to take fish in any of the fresh waters of this State by the method commonly known as angling with a hand line or rod and line, unless he has first procured a proper license and button therefor and unless at the time of the hunting, trapping, or fishing he has the license and button on his person and exhibits it for inspection to any warden, deputy warden, police officer, or other person requesting to see it."9

A bona-fide resident of this State, under the age of fourteen years, may without a license trap fur-bearing animals during the season when it is legal to do so. 39 These provisions have no application to the occupant of a farm in this State, actually residing thereon, or the immediate members of his family, but this exemption does not pertain to a person residing on the farm or in a tenant house thereon who is not a member of the occupant's family or to a servant of the occupant.99 

39. Same, R.S. 23:3-1. 136

A violation of this Act entails a penalty of twenty dollars and, if a license and button have been issued, the revocation thereof.40 The court or magistrate is to send the license, marked "revoked," to the office of the Board of Fish and Game Commissioners.40 

A license must be obtained in order to take oysters or clams from any of the natural oyster or clam grounds of this State." The license does not pertain to grounds reserved by the Board of Shell Fisheries for leasing purposes. 41 

§ 125. Fishing. In 1936 and 1937 the Legislature passed several acts for the protection of fish and fixed penalties for various actions.42 Fishing in the Delaware

River above or below the Trenton Falls is regulated by statute with certain penalties attached to offenses.48 

Regulations have likewise been made with respect to fishing in the Hudson River within the jurisdiction of this State.44

A 1939 statute prohibits the catching, taking, killing, or possessing of certain fish at specified times, the penalty for each fish unlawfully caught, taken, killed, or possessed being twenty dollars.a

The law provides that no person may take, catch, or kill on one day more than ten trout, ten pike perch, ten pickerel, twenty rock bass, twenty in the aggregate of crappie and calico bass, ten salmon, or ten in the aggregate of black bass and Oswego bass. Under no circumstances may any person take, catch, or kill on any one day more than twenty-five in the aggregate of fish commonly classed as fresh-water game and food fish.44b The

§ 126. Hunting. The law has forbidden such acts as the killing, catching, possessing, or selling of wild birds other than game birds, as defined in the statute, under a penalty of twenty dollars for each bird;45 further, the use of certain devices in hunting game birds is prohibited as is the hunting thereof except between sunrise and sunset under a penalty of twenty dollars for each offense.46

The hunting of certain animals by any means other than gun or bow and arrow and in the manner designated by statute has been made unlawful and penalized by a fine of twenty dollars for each offense. To hunt, pursue, shoot, shoot at, kill, capture, injure, or destroy a bird or animal, while in an automobile or by the aid or use of a light carried on, or attached to, an automobile, or to discharge any firearm on or across any state or county highway for the purpose of hunting, taking, or killing

The hunter may not, by moonlight or with the aid or by the use of any lamp, lantern, or artificial light, shoot or kill with a rifle of any kind or description or any firearm or shotgun a mink, muskrat, or otter, nor may he attempt to take the same except by means of a trap;50 likewise, he is forbidden to disturb or destroy any muskrat lodge or nesting chamber.50a One is not permitted to hunt, pursue, take, or trap such an animal at any time except from December 1st to March 15th of the following year;50b however, muskrats which are damaging dams or canal banks may be destroyed at any time by the owners or agents thereof." 50b 

Female English or ring-necked pheasants are protected by statute for a period of five years from July 4th, 1939, and no one may hunt, pursue, capture, kill, injure, or destroy them under a penalty of twenty dollars for each pheasant hunted, pursued, captured, killed, injured, or destroyed.5⁰c This provision does not apply to a person licensed under the Act or to one authorized by a licensee to shoot female pheasants on the lands described in the license. 50c

Steel traps may not be used in the trapping of fur-bearing animals without the permission of the owner, lessee, or person in possession of the land on which the trap is set.52 Provisions are made with regard to the setting of such traps.52 The penalty for each violation of the Act is twenty dollars.52 § 128. Shellfish. Regulations have been enacted with regard to the catching, selling, purchasing, dredging, or transportation of clams or oysters.53 54 Oysters may not be caught or taken by any means from any of the natural oyster beds under the tidal waters of the Atlantic seaboard of this State and the tributaries thereof, excepting the Delaware Bay, at any time other than during the months of May and June of each year and then only by duly licensed persons. This provision is not applicable to the Great Bay and Mullica

River, from which shellfish may be taken during the months of October, November, April, and May of each year, but the Board of Shell Fisheries may in its discretion close any or all of the beds during any or all of the months in this proviso mentioned for the purpose of protecting oyster seeds from excessive losses;54 the Board is to give at least fifteen days' notice of the closing of any or all of such beds.64 

Unless otherwise provided in the Shellfish Act, anyone violating any of the provisions of the Shellfish Act is

Forbidding Hunting, Fishing, or Trapping. The violation of any of these provisions results in a penalty of twenty dollars for each sign or notice." 74 § 129a. Coyotes. Section 130. Display. 

XXXIV. FLAGS 

§ 130. Display. The owner, proprietor, or lessee of any hall, place of amusement, auditorium, tent, or room used for the entertainment of the public or for public gatherings or meetings may not permit the holding of any entertainment or public gathering or meeting, unless the flag of the United States is publicly displayed in such place.76 One who is directly or indirectly in charge of any entertainment or public gathering or meeting on a public street or highway or lot or tract of land in any municipality may not proceed therewith, unless the flag of the United States, reasonably clean and in good repair, is publicly displayed." This statute, however, is not applicable to churches, auditoriums, or rooms used by congregations as places of religious worship.78 

The flag required by this Act is to be the standard flag of the United States and is to be of a size not less than fifty-two by sixty-six inches.79

He who violates any provision of this Act, on conviction thereof in a summary proceeding before a police recorder, magistrate, or justice of the peace, is to be sentenced to pay a fine of not more than one hundred dollars for each offense, for the use of the State.80

§ 131. Milk or Cream. It is unlawful to ship, transport, import, or offer for sale or resale or distribution to consumers or for manufacture as ice cream in this State, any milk or cream not produced by cows which have successfully passed an official tuberculin test.81 The penalty for the first violation is a fine of not less than one hundred dollars and not more than two hundred dollars and not more than two hundred dollars for each subsequent offense or imprisonment for a period not exceeding one year or both in the discretion of the court.82 Such penalties may be recovered in actions maintained by the state department of health.82

On the making of a complaint under oath or affirmation that a person has violated this Act, the district court, justice of the peace, police justice, or recorder of the municipality where the defendant resides or is apprehended may issue a summons or warrant for the defendant's arrest and may, when he is brought before the court, summarily hear the matter and determine his guilt or innocence without a jury.88

The following is a form for the complaint in these proceedings:

The aforesaid process may be in the nature of a summons or warrant, and the warrant may issue without the necessity of first obtaining a court order against the person so charged.90 If in the nature of a warrant, the process is returnable forthwith, while, if it be in the nature of a summons, it is returnable in not less than five and not more than fifteen entire days.90 The said summons or warrant must state the provision of the statute alleged to have been violated by the defendant or defendants.9⁰⁰ 

Defects and errors in the complaint and process may, at all times, be amended by the said court or judicial officer.9⁰⁰ Any officer authorized by law to serve and execute the process in the court wherein the action is brought is to serve and execute all the process in these proceedings.908

On the return of the aforesaid process or at any time to which the trial shall be adjourned, the court, without the filing of any pleadings and without a jury, proceeds summarily to hear the testimony and to give judgment for the plaintiff for the penalty and costs or to give judgment for the defendant.90f

Section 134. Hunting. 

XXXVI. FOXES 

135. Liberating Foxes. 

§ 134. Hunting. From the tenth day of November to the thirtieth day of April, one may not hunt foxes with hounds and firearms except in daylight, and foxes may not be hunted with hounds during the deer season.⁹6 

Twenty dollars is the penalty for a violation of this section.96 

To fail to report the killing of a fox within forty-eight hours to the warden of the county in which the fox has been killed or to the Board of Fish and Game Commissioners at Trenton results in a fine of five dollars." 97 

He who, while hunting foxes, kills, injures, destroys, or has in his possession any bird or animal the killing of which is prohibited is liable to a penalty of one hundred dollars for each bird or animal killed, injured, destroyed, or possessed.98 To have a live fox in possession in this 

State, except by the permission of the Board of Fish and Game Commissioners, entails a penalty of one hundred dollars for each live fox.99 

§ 135. Liberating Foxes. The penalty for each of fense of liberating a fox within this State is one hundred dollars.¹

XL. INFANTS Section 143. Avoidance of Obligations. 

144. Torts of Infant. 

145. Torts of Parent. 

§ 143. Avoidance of Obligations. An infant's note is voidable, unless the moneys he has borrowed thereon have been used for necessaries.13 He may avoid the transfer of certificates of deposit, especially in an instance where such certificates are held by one not a holder for value.14

The deed of an infant is merely voidable and not void, whether it is voluntary or is based on consideration.15 Should he elect to ratify the deed, he is bound by his election.15 His privilege to avoid the deed is a legal right, which continues to exist for twenty years after he has reached his majority.15 § 144. Torts of Infant. The general rule is that an infant is liable for his torts.16 The court has held recently that a parent may sue his emancipated child for a tort committed by the child."

When a minor has committed a tort, the action therefor lies against him and not against his guardian.18 "The statute provides that suits shall be brought by and against the infant in his own name and then he is authorized to prosecute or defend by his guardian appointed for that purpose." 18

§ 149. Search Warrants. A magistrate may issue a search warrant for the search of any premises, building, vehicle, or place containing, or believed on probable cause to contain, unlawful property under the Intoxicating Liquors Act;23 however, no warrant may issue for the search of any private dwelling, occupied exclusively as such, unless there is probable cause to believe that the dwelling is being used for, or in connection with, unlawful alcoholic-beverage activity and unless such use be evidenced by the oath of some person on his own knowledge.23

To permit the issuance of the warrant, there must be proof under oath, by written affidavit or by deposition, produced before the magistrate setting forth facts tending to establish the grounds for the application or probable cause to believe that such grounds exist and naming or describing the person or describing the premises, building, vehicle, or other place to be searched.24 On the 

22. 2 Rev. 1937, R.S. 33:1-26. 

23. Same, R.S. 33:1-56. 

24. 2 Rev. 1937, R.S. 33:1-57. 167

magistrate's being satisfied that the grounds for the application exist or that there is probable cause to believe that such grounds exist, he must issue a search warrant, signed by him with the title of his office, to any officer or officers, stating the particular grounds or the probable cause for its issuance and the name or names of the person or persons sworn in support thereof and commanding him forthwith to search the person or the premises, building, vehicle, or place to be searched.24 

The warrant is to be served by the officer or any of the officers to whom it is directed and by no other person except in aid of the said officer, he being present and acting in its execution. Such process must be executed and returned to the magistrate within forty-eight hours after its issuance, after which time the warrant, unless executed, is void.25

To procure the issuance and execution of a search warrant maliciously and without probable cause is to commit a misdemeanor. He who intentionally searches any private dwelling, occupied exclusively for dwelling purposes, without a warrant therefor or who maliciously and without probable cause and without a warrant therefor searches any other premises, building, vehicle, or place is guilty of a misdemeanor."

It is to be noted that the word "magistrate" under these provisions pertains, inter alia, to a police judge or justice or recorder of any municipality and all justices of the peace, but no justice of the peace may sit as a magistrate within the corporate limits of a municipality having a police judge or justice or recorder or a city criminal court or within the corporate limits of any municipality

151. Vacation. 

152. Pleading and Proof as Basis.

153a. The Satisfaction of Certain Judgments. 

§ 150. Court's Power over Judgments. The general rule has been said to be that, after the term during which a judgment has been regularly perfected, the court possesses no power to alter or disturb the judgment.29 

A defendant is entitled to such a judgment as will settle the plaintiffs' claims finally and will bind all the parties, so that no suit can thereafter be predicated on the same cause of action.29a 31 § 151. Vacation. A judgment which is void may be vacated at any time.30 When a judge concludes that he has erroneously entered a judgment, he must vacate it.³ 

After the vacating or setting aside of a judgment, the matter in controversy is open for a future determination.31

The time for the making of a motion to arrest the judgment is after the jury's verdict and before the judgment.31a

A judgment against a person under disability will not be vacated as a matter of right, unless the judgment is void.32 Such is the rule in the case of a judgment against

§ 153. Res Adjudicata. Res adjudicata, to constitute a valid defense to a cause of action, requires identity in the thing for which the suit has been brought as well as identity of cause of action, of the persons and parties to the action, and of the capacity of the parties for or against whom the claim is made, and the judgment in the former suit must be so in point as to control the issue in the pending proceeding. When the court has jurisdiction of the parties and of the subject-matter, the judgment is conclusive, not only as to the res in that case, but also as to all further litigation between the same parties concerning the same subject-matter even though the res may be different. The doctrine applies only to the issues necessarily litigated and decided in the first suit.38

A judgment cannot be said to be res adjudicata, unless it be binding on both parties to the second action.⁹ 

After a cause of action has been litigated between the parties, they "are estopped from re-litigating not only the issues actually raised and determined in the prior

suit, but also from raising and attempting to litigate any and all matters which could have been raised and presented for determination in that prior suit, even though they were not in fact so raised or considered therein." 39a

The exception to this rule has been said to be that "it is open to the parties in the second suit to raise and litigate the question of the jurisdiction of the court (in the first suit), to render or pronounce the final judgment, especially if that question was not actually litigated and expressly decided in the first suit.' "" 39a Unless the judgment is conclusive against a party, it is not conclusive in his favor.40 A judgment is presumptively conclusive against a party only in the character in which he has sued or has been sued.40 "One who was a party in his individual capacity is not bound by the judgment in another capacity, such as executor, trustee, or guardian, and vice versa."40

A cause of action merges in the final judgment based thereon, and, in the case of joint debts, that which merges the cause of action as to one debtor merges it as to all."¹ 

The court has held that "where several defendants are sued in one action for the breach of a joint and several contract or obligation, the entry of a final judgment against a defendant who has defaulted does not bar the prosecution of the action against the remaining defendant or defendants unless and until the judgment so entered has been satisfied."42 That a judgment has been recovered against one of several joint tortfeasors does not operate to satisfy the claim against those not sued, nor does the recovery operate as res adjudicata against the remaining tortfeasors; nevertheless, a payment dis- 43

XLIV. JURIES Section 154. Right to Trial by Jury. 

155. Qualifications. 

156. Exemptions from Jury Service. 

§ 154. Right to Trial by Jury. The test for determin ing whether a party is entitled to a jury trial rests in the performance of the conditions entitling him to such a trial and not in the ultimate destiny or responsibility for the loss of jury fees which have been advanced.45 "When the defendant made the necessary demand for the jury, paid the statutory fees, he performd the necessary acts which entitled him to have his case heard by a jury. His right to a jury became absolute and complete. It is not his concern what later became of the money. It is not difficult to visualize many circumstances" "such as loss, defalcation, theft, etc., which would make the moneys deposited and needed for a jury unavailable. But no such circumstances, other than a failure to comply with the terms of the act, or an express waiver thereof, could defeat defendant's right to a trial by jury."45 

§ 155. Qualifications. To be a grand or petit juror in any proceeding in any court of this State, one must be a citizen of this State for a period of not less than two years, over the age of twenty-one years and under the age of sixty-five years, and a resident of the county from which he is chosen; furthermore, he must not have been convicted of a crime and must not, at the time of his selection, be an official having directly or indirectly any interest in, or connection with, the administration of justice; he must be able to read and write the English lan- 46 

45. Tambe v. Otto, 113 N. J. L. 71, 172 Atl. Rep. 544. 

46. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 85, R.S. 2:85-1. 176

JURIES 

177 guage and must not have any physical disability which would prevent his properly performing his services as a juror.46 Women who possess these qualifications are eligible to serve as grand or petit jurors.46

No person whose name is not on the jury list may serve as a juror, if an objection on that ground is made prior to his being sworn or affirmed. This provision has no application to cases in which a foreign or struck jury or a tales de circumstantibus or a jury of view is ordered or to cases in which jurors are selected and drawn for a new panel on the discharge of the original panel as the result of a challenge to the array or for other good cause; for a new panel when a general panel has not been selected and drawn, or for a jury when a venire facias has been directed to the coroners or to the elisors for a jury to try a particular case.47 

§ 156. Exemptions from Jury Service. Every member of the military or naval forces of this State, on furnishing the proper authorities with a certificate to the effect that he has performed the duties required of him in a satisfactory manner for the year immediately preceding the receiving of a summons to act as a juror or during the period of his service if it be less than a year, is exempt from jury service. Such a member who has served for three years and has received a full and honorable discharge is exempt forever from all jury duty.48

47. Same, p. 86, R.S. 2:85-3. 48. 1 Rev. 1937, R.S. 2:86-2.

XLV. JURISDICTION Section 157. Acquiring Jurisdiction. 

158. Entry of Appearance. 

159. Consent. 

160. Jurisdictional Value. 

160a. Existence of District Court. 

§ 157. Acquiring Jurisdiction. A court can acquire jurisdiction over parties or over a res only by the issuance and service of a legal and valid writ or, in some instances, by the consent of the parties.49

When the court has jurisdiction, the judgment rendered is conclusive on the parties and their privies as to all the matters adjudged on which the parties have had a right to be heard.49a 

§ 158. Entry of Appearance. There is a presumption to the effect that an entry of appearance has been made with authority.50 However, the unauthorized entry of an appearance by a third party is ineffective.51 51a The effect of a general appearance is to waive any objection to the court's jurisdiction over the person." "A presumption very often exists in the practice of the law that the appearance of an attorney in a case on a motion, whether it is specially or general, will be presumed to be with authority until the presumption is rebutted." Where one of two defendants has not been served with process and has not filed an answer, his appearance by the solicitor who represents the other de-

§ 159. Consent. Jurisdiction over the subject-matter of a suit cannot be acquired by consent.53 It has been said that jurisdiction may not be conferred on the court by consent or assumed by acquiescence.54 55 

§ 160. Jurisdictional Value. The judgment of a court lacking the jurisdiction to enter the judgment is absolutely void; hence, when the judgment is in excess of the amount over which the court has jurisdiction, the judgment is void and not merely voidable.5

Since a void judgment may be attacked collaterally, a judgment of two hundred dollars, rendered by a justice of the peace in a municipality in which there is a district court, may be collaterally attacked in the district court.56 

Where the jurisdictional facts are apparent on the face of the proceedings, the judgment of a court of special and limited jurisdiction attains the same inviolability as that of a court of general jurisdiction.57 Should such facts not be apparent, the judgment is subject to collateral attack.57 

An action being brought for the balance due for services rendered, the plaintiff may waive the excess of the sum claimed over the jurisdictional amount.58 The test of the court's jurisdiction over the amount claimed lies in the balance in dispute between the parties to the action rather than in the amount of their claims against each other.58 It is not within the province of an appellate court to consider the alleged lack of jurisdiction in the

XLVI. LABOR Section 161. Hours for Females. 

162. Wage Collection. 

163. Insurance. 

164. Violations of Labor Act. 

§ 161. Hours for Females. Females may not be employed or permitted to work in any manufacturing establishment, bakery, or laundry before seven o'clock in the morning or after twelve o'clock in the evening.** This section does not apply to canneries engaged in the packing of perishable products such as fruits and vegetables, nor does the section apply to glass-manufacturing establishments.62

The violation of this Act by a corporation, its officers or agents, the members or agents of a firm, or any person incurs a penalty of not less than twenty-five dollars and not more than fifty dollars for the first offense and not less than fifty dollars and not more than two hundred dollars for each additional offense.63 64 

§ 162. Wage Collection. The Wage Collection Act of 

1934 vests an employee with certain extraordinary remedies to be used in collecting his wages, but the Act does not preclude an employee from instituting a suit on his claim in any court of competent jurisdiction, nor is the Act to be construed as denying or limiting the right of the plaintiff or the defendant to a trial by jury.65 When either party demands a trial by jury, he is to pay, at least two days before the return date or the adjourned 

62. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 355, R.S. 34:2-28. 

63. 2 Rev. 1937, R.S. 34:2-28.2. 

64. Same, R.S. 34:11-57 et seq. 

65. Same, R.S. 34:11-66. 181

date of the hearing of his cause, the statutory jury fee to the wage-collection division of the Department of Labor, whereupon such division files the entire record of the cause in a district court or in a justice's court in counties having no district court for the trial by jury.65 

This jury fee is to be paid to the court wherein the issue is to be tried by a jury with the judge or acting judge of the court presiding. The verdict of the jury with the judgment thereon are to be docketed in the court of common pleas.65 65 

§ 163. Insurance. The employer who fails to provide the protection of the compulsory insurance prescribed by law is guilty of a misdemeanor and, on conviction, is to be punished by a fine of not more than one thousand dollars or imprisonment for not more than sixty days or both.66 

§ 164. Violations of Labor Act. Unless it is otherwise specifically provided in the Labor Act, a penalty for the violation of any section of the Act is to be recovered by an action at law in the name of the Commissioner of Labor, to be instituted in any district court of a city or judicial district where the offense has been committed or in the recorders' courts of towns, boroughs, or cities where the offense has been committed or in the court of a justice of the peace having due jurisdiction." 67 The first process is the summons, which is to be made returnable not less than five days from the date thereof and is to specify a certain time and place for the defendant's appearance. Service of the summons on an individual may be made by serving him personally or by leaving a copy of the process at his dwelling house or place in the presence of some person of the family of the age 

65. Same, R.S. 34:11-66. 

66. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 373, R.S. 34:15-79. 

67. 2 Rev. 1937, R.S. 34:1-70.

183 67 of fourteen years or more, who is informed of the contents thereof. Should the person violating the Act be the owner or operator of the business in which the offense has been committed, service may then be made on the manager, superintendent, foreman, or person in charge of the busniess. Service on a corporation may be made on a president, vice-president, secretary, or any director, registered agent, or other officer, and, if none resides in the county wherein the offense has been committed or if one cannot be found therein, service may be made on the manager, superintendent, foreman, or person in charge of the business where the offense has been committed.67

The proceedings thereafter are the same as those in an action at law in such court. The court's finding is to be that the defendant has or has not, as the case may be, incurred the penalty claimed in the demand of the plaintiff, and judgment is entered accordingly."

Should an execution issue and be returned unsatisfied, the court, on application and after notice to the defendant, may award an execution to take the body of the defendant if he be an individual.67 In case the defendant is committed under such an execution, he may not be discharged under the insolvency laws of this State and may be discharged only by the court making the order for the body execution or by one of the Justices of the Supreme Court, when the court or the Justice is satisfied that further confinement will not result in the payment of the judgment and costs.67

XLVII. LANDLORD AND TENANT Section 165. Jurisdiction. 

166. Causes for Removal of Tenant. 

68 

§ 165. Jurisdiction. In dispossession proceedings, the court cannot consider facts not set forth in the affidavit; hence, when the affidavit consist merely of a conclusion of law and fails to disclose a landlord-tenant relationship between the parties, the court has no jurisdiction.68 When the tenant enters no appearance in the proceedings and does not defend and when there is no variance as to the landlord-tenant relationship, the description of the premises, or the specific amount due stated in the affidavit, the fact that the affidavit alleges the leasing of the premises to two tenants and the proof discloses a leasing merely to one is no reason to dismiss the proceedings. A defendant who, appearing in court, pleads and then examines witnesses waives the right to object to the court's jurisdiction on the ground of the alleged insufficiency of the affidavit.70 69 The affidavit in these proceedings must allege the true amount of the rent due;" the tenant may pay the rent plus the costs and, thus, preclude the proceedings from continuing. 71 The court's right to assume jurisdiction over the action by a mortgagee in possession to dispossess tenants for the failure to pay rent depends on whether the facts alleged show a landlord-tenant relationship." § 166. Causes for Removal of Tenant. A lessee or ten ant at will or at sufferance or for a part of a year or for one or more years of any houses, lands, or tenements and the assigns, under-tenants, or legal representatives of such a lessee or tenant may be removed from the premises by any district court in the county wherein the premises are located, or, if the premises do not lie in a county where there exists a district court, the removal may be made by any justice of the peace of such county in the manner hereinafter prescribed and in the following cases: 78 1. Where the person holds over and continues in possession of all or any part of the premises after the expiration of his term and after a demand has been made and written notice has been given by the landlord or his agent for the delivery of the possession thereof, which notice is to be served either personally on the tenant or the person in possession by giving him a copy of the notice or by leaving him a copy at his usual place of abode with a member of his family over the age of fourteen years, or, if for any reason such service cannot be made, the notice may be served by posting or affixing a copy thereof on the door or other conspicuous part of such dwelling or demised premises; 

2. Where the person holds over after a default in the payment of the rent, pursuant to the agreement under which the premises are held, or 3. Where the person is so disorderly as to destroy the peace and quiet of the other tenants living in the house

XLVIII. LEGAL HOLIDAYS Section 167. Enumeration and Effect. 

§ 167. Enumeration and Effect. The following days and half days are, for all purposes so far as concerns the presenting for payment or acceptance and the protesting and giving notice of dishonor of bills of exchange, bank checks, and promissory notes, to be treated and considered as Sunday and a public holiday or half holiday:" 

New Year's Day, Lincoln's Birthday, Washington's Birth day, Good Friday, Decoration Day, Independence Day, Labor Day, Columbus Day, Armistice Day, Christmas

Day, any general election day in this State, every Saturday in the months of July and August as well as every Saturday in the months of September to June, both inclusive, from twelve o'clock noon until twelve o'clock midnight which is a half holiday, and any day appointed, ordered, or recommended by the Governor of this State or by the President of the United States as a day of thanksgiving or fasting or prayer or other religious observance or as a bank holiday or holidays." When such a day falls on a Sunday, the Monday next following is deemed to be, for all of the aforesaid purposes, a public holiday. All of the afore-mentioned days, excepting bank holidays and Saturdays in the months of July and August, are to be considered as Sundays and holidays or half holidays so far as regards the transaction of business in the public offices of this State or of the counties of this State.74

It is to be noted that certain statutes provide that the proceedings thereunder may be instituted and the process served on Sundays. The reader may see the various subjects treated in this book on that point. 

74. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 377, R.S. 36:1-1. 188

XLIX. LIENS Section 168. Charitable Institutions. 

169. Physicians. 

170. Hotels, Inns, Etc. 

171. Processors. 

§ 168. Charitable Institutions. A charitable institu tion, maintained in whole or in part by state or county funds, has a lien against the property of persons confined therein and receiving care and treatment." The said lien, when properly recorded, possesses priority over all unrecorded encumbrances.75 The form of the lien is prescribed by the State Department of Institutions and Agencies.76 

Additional liens may be filed from time to time with respect to subsequent accumulations of delinquent maintenance until the patient is no longer receiving care and treatment in the institution.76 The lien may be filed at any time during the period of hospitalization.76 

Should it be discovered that a patient is possessed of any goods, rights, credits, chattels, moneys, or effects which are held by any person, firm, or corporation for the present or subsequent use of the patient, the aforementioned lien or a notice of the existence thereof may be forwarded by registered mail to the person, firm, or corporation and, thus, become binding on the property rights so held." Thereafter such property rights may not be disposed of by the holder theerof until the lien has been satisfied or the owner of the lien consents to the disposition." For the person, firm, or corporation to dispose of the moneys or property after receiving the notice of

the lien is to be liable to the institution for the value of such moneys or property."

A compromise may be made in order to settle such a lien.78

This Act does not prevent the institution's recovering for maintenance which has been furnished but is not secured by a lien.79 

§ 169. Physicians. The law gives a licensed physician in this State a lien for the reasonable charges for his services rendered in the treatment of a person who has sustained personal injuries in an accident as the result of the negligence or alleged negligence of another.80 Such charges, however, are to be based on the usual fees and charges prevailing in the same community for similar services.8 80 It is to be noted that these provisions do not apply to accidents or injuries within the purview of the 

Workmen's Compensation Law.81 

The lien under discussion attaches to any and all rights of actions, claims, suits, and demands as well as to any judgment, award, or determination and to the proceeds of any settlement of such rights of action, suits, claims, demands, judgments, awards, or determinations which the injured person may or shall have, assert, or maintain against the other party on account of damages for the injuries, until the date of the payment of such damages. 

Irrespective of the amount of the aforesaid reasonable charges, all of such liens for services rendered to any one person as the result of any one accident may not exceed twenty-five per centum of the amount of the award, verdict, report, decision, decree, judgment, or settlement.88

191 The means of perfecting the lien and of realizing thereon are outlined in the statute.83 § 170. Hotels, Inns, Etc. The law gives a lien to any hotel proprietor, apartment-hotel, inn, and boardinghouse proprietor on all the baggage or other property belonging to any guest or brought on the premises by any guest, tenant, boarder, or lodger in a hotel, apartment hotel, inn, or boarding house to the extent of the amount of his bill for the rent or hire of rooms, board, lodging, cash advanced, or other accomodations furnished in the said hotel, apartment hotel, inn, or boarding house.84 

Such a proprietor has the right, without legal process, to detain the property until the debt is discharged. 84 Provision is made for the procedure to be taken in realizing on the lien.85

It would be well to note that the proprietor of a hotel, apartment house, inn, or boarding house is not liable in any sum to any guest, tenant, boarder, or lodger for the loss of wearing apparel, goods, merchandise, or other property, unless it shall appear that the loss has occurred through the fault or negligence of the proprietor, and, further, the proprietor is not liable in any sum for the loss of any article or articles of wearing apparel, trunk, satchel, valise, bag, box, bundle, or the contents thereof, or any property belonging to such guest, tenant, lodger, or boarder in the hotel, apartment hotel, inn, or boarding house and not within the room or rooms assigned to him, unless such property has been specially entrusted to the care and custody of the proprietor or his duly authorized agent.85 If he has been so specially entrusted with the said property of the guest or traveler, the proprietor is not liable for the loss thereof in any sum exceeding one hundred dollars.85

§ 171. Processors. A processor who has done or furnished any film-laboratory work, labor, services, or materials possesses a lien on the motion-picture films and on all other motion-picture films of the owner and of all parties having any authority with respect to such films, negative or negatives, or positive print or prints.86 The processor may retain possession thereof until the payment of the indebtedness due to him therefor.86 Such lien is not waived or impaired by the extension of credit or by the taking of any note or other instrument for the payment of the money due or by the taking of any security for the payment thereof or by the recovery of a judgment therefor.87

The Act then prescribes the procedure to be taken in realizing on the lien.88

L. LIMITATIONS, STATUTE OF Section 172. Operation of Statute. 

173. Acknowledgment of Liability. 

174. Libel and Slander. 

175. Amended Pleadings. 

176. Necessity of Pleading. 

§ 172. Operation of Statute. The statute of limita tions starts to run, not from the date the obligation has existed, but rather from the date of the accrual of the action.89 "The accrual of the cause of action is under our state statute the prescribed event from which the period of limitations is to be computed. By the accrual of the cause of action is to be understood the right to institute and maintain a suit."8⁹a With respect to a cause of action based on a professional man's negligent performance of his duty, the statute commences to run from the occurrence of the breach of duty and not from the time of the discovery of the actual damages resulting from the breach.90 When the statute of limitations has once begun to run, "its course will not be impeded or its operation suspended by any subsequent disability.”⁹1 

§ 173. Acknowledgment of Liability. So far as the running of the statute of limitations is concerned, the acknowledgement of liabiltiy by the maker of a note does not operate to render the guarantor thereof liable.** 

§ 174. Libel and Slander. Civil actions for libel and slander must be commenced and sued within one year 

89. White v. Liskovsky, 17 N. J. Misc. 8, 3 Atl. Rep. (2d) 123.

90. Sullivan v. Stout, 120 N. J. L. 304, 199 Atl. Rep. 1. 

91. Dalton v. Hoboken, 12 N. J. Misc. 216, 171 Atl. Rep. 141. 

92. Marinelli v. Lombardi, 16 N. J. Misc. 71, 196 Atl. Rep. 701. 

[Honeyman's Justice Supp.]-13 193

next after the publication of the alleged libel or slander and not thereafter.93 § 175. Amended Pleadings. Generally an amended complaint will not be held to state a new cause of action and will, therefore, not be barred by the statute of limitations, if the allegations disclose substantially the same wrong with respect to the same transaction or if the same matter is more fully or differently stated of if the gist of the action or the subject-matter of the litigation remains the same, even though the form of the liability asserted or the incidents of the transaction may be different.94 

Technical rules will not be applied in determining whether the transactions are identical.94

However, an entirely new and different cause of action cannot be set forth after the statute of limitations has barred the cause. 95 

§ 176. Necessity of Pleading. The operation of the statute of limitations does not deprive the court of jurisdiction over the cause but constitutes a defense, which must be affirmatively pleaded.96 The defense of the statute is not available unless it is pleaded."

LI. MARRIAGES Section 177. Power to Perform Ceremony. 

177a. Common-Law Marriages. 

178. Syphilis Test. 

§ 177. Power to Perform Cermony. Recorders and police justices possess the power, in the eyes of the law, to solemnize marriages between such persons as may lawfully enter into the matrimonial relation.98 

§ 177a. Common-Law Marriages. Common-law mar riages are no longer valid in this State.98a A marriage contracted on or after December 1st, 1939, is not valid unless the contracting parties have obtained a marriage license and unless the marriage has been performed by or before a duly authorized person, religious society, institution, or organization.98a To render the marriage valid, there must be both the license and the ceremony. 

§ 178. Syphilis Test. To be eligible to obtain a mar riage license, one must obtain from a qualified physician a certificate to the effect that the applicant has undergone the Wassermann or Kahn or other similar standard laboratory blood test and that, in the physician's opinion, the applicant either is not infected with syphilis or is not in a stage of that disease—which may become communicable.99

Any applicant for a marriage license, physician, or representative of a laboratory who misrepresents any fact called for by "the certificate form" prescribed by the Syphilis Test Act or any licensing officer who fails to receive "the certificate form" or who has reason to believe

that any of the facts therein has been so misrepresented and nevertheless issues a marriage license and any person who otherwise fails to comply with the provisions of the said Act is subject to a penalty of not less than ten dollars and not more than one hundred dollars.¹ This penalty may be recovered with costs in an action of debt by, and in the name of, the local board of health of the municipality where the license has been issued or by, and in the name of, the Department of Health of the .¹

LII. MOTOR FUEL Section 179. Licenses and Records. 

180. Price Signs. 

181. Transportation. 

§ 179. Licenses and Records. One who engages in business as a retail or wholesale dealer in motor fuels, without procuring a license from the State Tax Commissioner, is subject to a penalty of one hundred dollars and, on failure to pay such penalty after conviction, to imprisonment for a period not exceeding thirty days.2

Such a dealer's failure to keep the records required by the Act or his failure or refusal to permit the inspection of the records by the Commissioner or any of his agents results in a penalty of not more than one hundred dollars for each offense and, in default of the payment of the penalty, imprisonment forthwith for a period not exceeding thirty days.³ § 180. Price Signs. A retail dealer who fails to post and publicly display, in the manner required by the Act, a sign stating the price per gallon of all motor fuel sold by him or who sells motor fuel at a price other than the per-gallon price as provided in the Act or who violates any other provision of the second article of the Act is subject to a penalty, on conviction thereof, of not less than fifty dollars and not more than two hundred dollars for each offense and, in default of the payment of the penalty, to imprisonment for a period of not more than thir- 

2. 2 Rev. 1937, R.S. 54:39–51. 

3. Same, R.S. 54:39-52. · 197

ty days. A conviction of a second or subsequent offense may result in a revocation of the dealer's license.*

The aforesaid penalty is to be sued for in name of the State Tax Commissioner. Every district court, police justice, recorder, justice of the peace, or other police magistrate is authorized, on the filing of a written and duly verified complaint by the Commissioner or by any of his assistants or employees, which complaint may be made on information or belief, that a retail dealer has violated the second article of the Act, to issue process at the suit of the Commissioner as plaintiff."

If the process is in the nature of a warrant, it is returnable forthwith, and, if it is in the nature of a summons, it is returnable in not less than five days and not more than ten days. The process must specify the section of the Act alleged to have been violated. It is to be served and executed by the officers authorized to serve the process of the said court.5 

The court has the power to adjourn the hearing or trial from time to time, but, excepting in an instance where the first process has been a summons, the judicial officer must detain the defendant in safe custody, unless he enters into a bond to the Commissioner, with at least one sufficient surety, in a sum fixed by the court,-which is to be not less than fifty dollars and not more than two hundred dollars, conditioned for his appearance on the day to which the hearing is adjourned and thence from day to day until the disposition of the cause and then to abide by the judgment of the court.5 This bond, should it be forfeited, may be prosecuted by the Commissioner." 

On the return of the process or at the time to which the trial may be adjourned, the court proceeds and summarily hears the testimony and, without the filing of any pleadings, determines the matter and gives judgment, without a jury, either for the plaintiff for the recovery of the penalty with costs or for the defendant." 

5. 1938 Stat. Serv., 395, R.S. 39:12-4.

Should the judgment be in the plaintiff's favor, the court is to cause the defendant who may refuse or fail forthwith to pay the judgment and the costs and charges incident thereto to be committed to the county jail for a term not exceeding the above-mentioned period." 

The conviction under this statute is to be in the following or similar form:5

County of Camden SS. 

Be it remembered, that on this 21st day of June, 1939, in said

County, Joseph A. Smith, the defendant, was by the recorder's court in the Borough of Audubon convicted of violating Section ...... of 

Article II of an act entitled "An act to regulate the retail sale of motor fuels, and providing penalties for violation," approved May 12th and June 22d, 1938, in a summary proceeding at the suit of the 

State Tax Commissioner, upon a complaint by Henry Simmons; and, further, that the witnesses in said proceeding who testified for the plaintiff were (name them), and the witnesses who testified for the defendant were (name them).

Wherefore, the said court does hereby give judgment that the plaintiff recover of the defendant dollars penalty and 

dollars costs of this proceeding. 

Such conviction is to be signed by the judicial officer, and, when the defendant is committed to jail in default of the payment of the penalty, a commitment in the following form is to be added beneath the signature to the conviction:5 

And the said Joseph A. Smith, neglecting and refusing to pay the amount of the penalty above mentioned, with costs, it is hereby ordered that the said Joseph A. Smith be and he is hereby committed to the common jail of the County of Camden for a period of days, unless the said penalty and cost are sooner paid. 

This commitment is signed by the judge, and, should the defendant be committed to jail, the conviction and the commitment are then signed in duplicate." One of the duplicate copies serves the purpose of a warrant of commitment. Where the defendant serves the full period 

5. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 395, R.S. 39:12-4.

MOTOR FUEL 

201 for which he has been committed, he is entitled on being released to have the judgment satisfied of record; the certificate of the jail warden that the defendant has been detained for the period specified in the commitment is sufficient warrant for the clerk of any court in which the judgment is docketed to discharge it of record. 

The clerk of the district court, recorder's court, or police court may sign and seal any process issued under this Act other than a warrant of commitment.5

An execution may be issued for the collection of the judgment against the goods and chattels and the body of the defendant without first obtaining an order for that purpose.

The costs in these proceedings are the same as those taxed in actions in the court and are recoverable by the Commissioner in the event of the conviction of the defendant.5 

§ 181. Transportation. The owner or operator of any conveyance used in transporting or hauling motor fuel who fails to have in his possession, at all times while doing so, a delivery ticket in accord with the provisions of the Act is to be fined fifty dollars or imprisoned for not more than twenty days for each offense."

The proceedings to recover these penalties are the same as those set forth in the preceding section with the following two exceptions: the process, if in the nature of a summons, is returnable in not less than five days and not more than fifteen days," and the bond, which may be given by the defendant in order to obtain his freedom, after an adjournment of the case, must be in double the amount of the penalty.

LIII. MOTOR VEHICLES.

11 § 182. Registration and Licenses. No one may drive a motor vehicle on a public highway of this State, unless he be duly licensed to do so.¹¹ To be eligible to obtain a license, one must not be under the age of seventeen years and one must have passed a satisfactory examination as to his ability as an operator." A violation of this Act results in a fine of not more than five hundred dollars or imprisonment in the county jail for a period of not more than sixty days."1

All motor vehicles are required to be registered, and a vehicle may not be driven unless it has been registered in accord with the Act.12 The registration expires and the certificate thereof becomes void on the thirty-first day of each March.12 To violate this Act is to be liable to a penalty of not more than one hundred dollars.12 

The driver or operator of a motor vehicle must, at all times when he is in charge of the vehicle on the highways, have in his possession his driver's license and the registration certificate.18 These papers must be exhibited 

11. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 383, R.S. 39:3-10. 

12. 1938 Stat. Serv., p. 382, R.S. 39:3-4. 

13. 2 Rev. 1937, R.S. 39:3-29. 203

13 on the request of a motor-vehicle inspector, police officer, or magistrate acting in the performance of his duties." 

The driver or operator must write his name in the presence of the officer, so that the officer may determine the identity of the licensee and the correctness of the registration certificate.13 A fine of not more than one hundred dollars is the penalty for a violation of this section.13 

Provisions have been enacted for the registration of motor vehicles owned by manufacturers or dealers as well as for the registration of omnibusses, penalties being attached to violations.¹4

To operate a conveyance for the purpose of transporting or hauling fuels without having the proper license card displayed on the conveyance or to operate a conveyance without having license plates thereon results in a fine of one hundred dollars and, for failure or refusal to pay such fine forthwith after conviction, imprisonment for not more than ninety days.15

When a person's license or registration certificate has been revoked or suspended and he fails to return one or both to the Commissioner of Motor Vehicles, together with any registration plates issued under such certificate, within five days of the date of the revocation or suspension or when he fails to surrender one or both of them on the demand of a motor-vehicle inspector, member of the state police, or other police officer who has been directed to secure possession thereof, such person is to be fined not more than twenty-five dollars.¹ 16 § 182a. § 183. Identification Marks. The identification marks must be displayed on a motor vehicle in accord with the Act." Any violation of this provision is punishable by a fine of not more than one hundred dollars and, in default of the payment of the fine, by imprisonment in the county jail for not more than ten days." In the case of a second conviction for the same violation, there may be imposed a fine in double the said amount and, in default of the payment thereof, imprisonment for not more than twenty days.17

These penalties do not apply in the case of the display of a fictitious number, when the punishment consists of a fine of not more than five hundred dollars or imprisonment in the county jail for a period of not more than sixty days.17

§ 186. Unauthorized Operation. To operate or use any motor vehicle without the permission of the owner thereof entails a fine of not less than one hundred dollars.20 

§ 186a. Lawful Rates of Speed. Subject to the provi sions of the law as to reckless driving and with the exceptions of those instances where a lower speed is required, it is prima facie lawful for the driver of a vehicle to drive it at a rate not exceeding the following rates under the circumstances given:20a 

1. Ten miles an hour, when passing a school during recess or while children are going to or leaving school during opening or closing hours; 

2. Fifteen miles an hour, when approaching within fifty feet and in traversing an intersection of highways and when the driver's view is obstructed; his view is deemed to be obstructed, when, at any time during the last fifty feet of his approach to the intersection, he does not have a clear and uninterrupted view of the intersection and of the traffic on all the highways entering the intersection, for a distance of two hundred feet from the intersection; 

3. Fifteen miles an hour, when traversing or going around curves or traversing a grade on a highway and when the driver's view is obstructed within a distance of one hundred feet along the highway in the direction in which he is proceeding; 

4. Twenty miles an hour on a highway in a business district, when traffic on the highway is controlled at intersection by traffic officers or traffic signals; 

19. Same, R.S. 39:8-9. 

20. Same, R.S. 39:4-48. 

20a. 1939 Stat. Serv., p. 263, R.S. 39:4-98.

MOTOR VEHICLES 

207 5. Fifteen miles an hour on all other highways in a business district; 

6. Twenty miles an hour in a residence district; 

7. Forty miles an hour under all other conditions except as hereinafter stated, and 

8. Forty-five miles an hour for passenger vehicles only within zones designated by the Commissioner of Motor Vehicles, who is vested with the authority to designate any part of any highway under the jurisdiction and control of the State Highway Department, where a speed of forty miles an hour is permitted, as a zone in which it is prima facie lawful for the driver of a passenger vehicle to drive at a speed of not more than forty-five miles an hour, 20a

However, in any instance where such a speed would be unsafe, it is not lawful.20a 

§ 187. Non-Resident Drivers. The touring privileges allowed by our laws to a non-resident are extended to any non-resident chauffeur or driver who has complied with the law of his own state or country as to licenses.21 

A non-resident may not operate a motor vehicle registered under the laws of this State, unless he be seventeen years of age or over, nor may he be permitted to avail himself of the right of driving a New Jersey registered vehicle unless he holds a driver's license from the state or country in which he resides.21 

At all times while operating a motor vehicle in this State under the reciprocity provision, he must have in his possession the registration certificate of the car he is then operating as well as his driver's license, and he is to exhibit them to any motor-vehicle inspector, police officer, or magistrate who, in the performance of his duty, asks to see them.21 

20a. 1939 Stat. Serv., p. 263, R.S. 39:4-98. 

21. 1939 Stat. Serv., p. 260, R.S. 39:3-17.

A violation of this Act results in a fine of not more than five hundred dollars or imprisonment in the county jail for a period of not more than sixty days.21

With respect to the service of process on non-resident drivers through the Commissioner of Motor Vehicles, the reader may see the title Process in this book. 

§ 188. Transportation. The operators of autobusses commonly called jitneys, school busses carrying school children, commercial motor vehicles carrying explosive substances or inflammable liquids or fuel oils as a cargo or as part of a cargo, commercial motor vehicles carrying persons as passengers, or any motor vehicles moving in units of two or more coupled together must, before crossing the tracks of any railroad or high-speed interurban railroad at grade, bring the vehicle to a full stop and shift the gears thereof to a neutral position.22 Such stop is to be made at a point where the maximum vision of the crossing in both directions can be obtained, providing that such point is not more than fifty feet from the nearest rail.22

A violation of this Act is punishable by a fine of not more than fifty dollars for the first offense and for the second offense a fine of not more than one hundred dollars or imprisonment for not more than thirty days or both.22

On summary conviction by a court of competent jurisdiction of a violation of the statute with respect to the number of hours the operator of a commercial motor vehicle may work, a person is to be sentenced to pay a fine of twenty-five dollars for the first offense and, in default of the payment thereof, is to be imprisoned for a period of not more than five days.23 For each subsequent offense the fine is fifty dollars and the imprison-

In the prosecution for a violation of the Motor Vehicle Act, the complaint must be made before the warrant can be issued; however, the jurat to the complaint need not be signed prior thereto.24 The clerk of a police justice's court possesses the authority to take a complaint based on the driving of a motor vehicle while under the influence of liquor, even though the arrest has been made without a warrant by the police officer in whose presence the offense has occurred.25 No prejudice to the defendant may be found in the fact that the clerk has acted in the name of the magistrate and not in his own name.25

When the defendant gives a bond for his appearance in a prosecution under the Motor Vehicle Act, the recorder thereby acquires jurisdiction; hence, the fact that the warrant has never been served is immaterial.26 

The taking of a formal plea in such a proscution has been held to be unnecessary. 24

LIV. MUNICIPALITIES AND COUNTIES Section 191. Injuries on Public Property. 

192. Liability for Acts. 

193. Employment. 

194. Audit of Accounts. 

195. Disobeying Subpoena. 

§ 191. Injuries on Public Property. A municipality is not liable for any injury to a person resulting from the use of any public grounds, buildings, or structures,28 nor is there any such liability on the part of a school district.29 

§ 192. Liability for Acts. Where there is no active wrongdoing, municipalities are not subject to liability for acts committed in the performance of governmental functions.30 "A municipality is not answerable for nonfeasance on the part of its servants or agents unless such liability be created by positive statutory law, but a municipality is liable to an individual who suffers a private wrong when such injury arises from the misfeasance or malfeasance of the municipality when that private wrong results from active wrongdoing on the part of the municipality, and even though it be in the performance of a governmental duty under circumstances and results for which an indictment would lie." 31

The active wrongdoing on which the liability of a municipal corporation may be predicated must be an act of positive misfeasance as distinguished from mere negligence.81a The private action must rest on a positive, affirmative act which is wrongful in itself and detrimental

31a. Robinson v. Ocean Township, 9 Atl. Rep. (2d) 300. 211

to the plaintiff.31a Misfeasance, in this connection, has been said to be a wrongful and injurious exercise of lawful authority or the doing of a lawful act in an unlawful manner.31a 32 

§ 193. Employment. An appointing officer of the 

State or of a county or municipality may not, in employing people, discriminate against an applicant on the ground that the applicant has attained the age of at least forty years. However, this Act does not apply to appointments to the police or fire departments or to the position of court attendants, process server, and prison or reformatory officer or guard in houses of detention, jails, county farms, and penal institutions.32 violation of this Act is a misdemeanor.32 

§ 194. Audit of Accounts. A person who makes, or begins to make, an audit of municipal or county accounts as required by this Act, contrary to the provisions of the Act or without a license therefor in full force and effect, is liable to a penalty of one hundred dollars for every audit so made.38 Such penalty is to be recovered by an action at law instituted by the State Board of Public 

Accountants in a court of competent jurisdiction.³ 

§ 195. Disobeying Subpoena. In proceedings for the removal or detention of policemen or firemen, a person who refuses or neglects to obey the command of a writ of subpoena, issued by the board, officer, or authority having the power to try and determine the matter, is liable to a penalty of twenty-five dollars, to be sued for in the corporate name of the municipality in any court of competent jurisdiction. The penalty is to be paid into the poor fund or, should there be none, into the general fund of the municipality.34 34

LV. NIGHT POLICE COURT Section 196. Creation. 

197. Powers. 

198. Clerks and Employees. 

§ 196. Creation. The governing body of a first-class city may, by means of an ordinance, create a court to be known as the night police court.35 Such court is presided over by a judge appointed by a resolution of the governing body for a period concurrent with the term of office for which the governing body has been elected.35 

The night police court is a court of record and possesses an official seal. This arm of the judiciary sits nightly on the nights designated by the governing body in its discretion 37 and at such places and for such lengths of times as that body provides.38 36 The governing body may, by resolution, fix the judge's annual salary, which is to be paid in the same manner as those of the other officers of the city.39 § 197. Powers. The night police court is to have, possess, and exercise all of the power, authority, and jurisdiction of the criminal courts in first-class cities and is to have concurrent jurisdiction with the said criminal courts.40 Such power, authority, and jurisdiction are vested in the judge of the court." Likewise, he possesses the power to preside in any other police court in and for the municipality, wherein he has been appointed, at

LVI. PEDDLERS 200. Cancellation of Licenses. 

§ 199. Licenses. The following individuals have the right to hawk, peddle, and vend any wares, goods, or merchandise or solicit trade within this State, by procuring a license for that purpose in accord with this Act:"7 honorably discharged soldiers, sailors, marines, nurses, or army field clerks of the United States, who have been in the military or naval service of the United States in any war in which this country has been engaged prior to the World War or in the Boxer uprising, the Philippine insurrection, the Mexican expedition, or in any Indian wars or uprising, or in the World War prior to 

November 11, 1918, the date of the Armistice, and who have been residents of this State; exempt members of a volunteer fire department, volunteer fire engine, hook and ladder, hose, supply company, or salvage corps of any municipality or fire district in this State, who hold exemption certificates issued to them as exempt members of any such department, company, or corps and who are residents of this State.47

No license may be issued to any person in the first group mentioned above for hawking, peddling, and vending emblems, flags, natural or artificial flowers, magazines, pamphlets, postcards, or any printed matter, or any article emblematic or symbolic of, or referring to, the army, navy, marine corps, or any veterans' association, unless the licensee has been a bona-fide resident of the county wherein he resides for the three months 

47. 2 Rev. 1937, R.S. 45:24-9. 215

48 immediately preceding the issuance of the license. Such a license is effective only in the county wherein the licensee resides.4 48 200. Cancellation of Licenses. After due notice and a hearing, a city magistrate or recorder may order the cancellation of any license issued to a member of the first group mentioned in the preceding section-which has been sold or transferred by the original licensee. The magistrate or recorder is to mail the order of cancellation to the county clerk in whose county such license has been issued, whereupon the county clerk cancels the same of record and files the order of cancellation in his office and then sends notice of the cancellation to the office of the adjutant general."9

When such a licensee sells or transfers his license, he is guilty of a misdemeanor.

§ 202. Engineers. In case of a violation of the Act with respect to the licenses of engineers, the State Board of Professional Engineers and Land Surveyors or any member or officer thereof may prefer a complaint for the violation before any court, tribunal, or magistrate possessing jurisdiction and may by its officers, counsel, and agents aid in presenting the laws or facts to such court, tribunal, or magistrate.51 It is the duty of the county prosecutor to prosecute all such violations.51 

§ 203. Unemployment Compensation. Under the Un employment Compensation Act, penalties may be recovered for various wrongful acts." 52 

§ 204. Corporate Tax Statement. Any street-railway, traction, gas and electric-light, heat, or power corporation neglecting or refusing to make the tax statement prescribed by the Act relative thereto is to be penalized the sum of one hundred dollars per day for each day of

§ 206. Service within Jurisdiction. The general rule may be said to be that process must be served personally within the court's jurisdiction on the person to be affected thereby.57

The usual place of abode for the service of process is the place where the defendant is actually living at the time of the service." 58 

§ 207. Issuance of Summons. There exists a presump tion, in the absence of proof to the contrary, that a summons has been issued on the day it bears date.59

A party may waive his right to have a suit against him begun by process.59a 

§ 208. Substituted Service. A statute permitting sub stituted service of process is in derogation of the common law and must be strictly construed and fully carried out

The exemption from the service of civil process extended to those going to, attending, and returning from, judicial proceedings in any court of this State pertains to a non-resident and not to a resident.81 Residence in this connection is synonymous with domicile.81 The exemption does not pertain to a non-resident who is in this
